**Explicitly deferred from Feature #9 Phase 1** (not started): event-driven triggers, any second condition type or field, any action beyond notify/create-task, multi-step/sequenced nurturing, per-lead workflow-state tracking, a rules-management UI, a visual workflow builder, a generic action dispatcher, any change to `LeadScoringRule`, Client User visibility into nurturing configuration (existing Task/Notification visibility rules govern who sees results, unchanged), the pre-existing `SendLeadAlertNotifications`/`SendTaskAlertNotifications` JSON-query fix noted above.

**Verification**: 26 new tests, all passing (`LeadNurturingTest`) — settings-toggle independence, Client User denial, threshold validation (negative rejected, 366 rejected, exactly 365 accepted), exact-threshold-boundary qualification and one-day-under exclusion, merged-lead exclusion, closed-lead exclusion, open-lead eligibility, tenant isolation, recipient correctness (admin + assignee, no duplicate when the same person is both), same-day notification and task dedup, notification payload correctness, task field correctness (title/due date/priority/status/assignment/`created_by`), `TaskAssignedNotification` reuse, the `updated_at`-preservation guarantee, per-lead and per-tenant failure isolation, command registration, and a regression check that the three existing alert settings are unaffected. Full `tests/Feature/Leads` + `Notifications` + `Broadcasting`: 751/754 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same `LeadsIndexListenerTest` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. Full application suite: 863/866 passed — same 2 pre-existing failures, same 1 pre-existing skip, zero new failures. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 352 vs. the 337 baseline established at Feature #8's closure — the +15 traced individually and reproducibly to the same already-accepted `Auth::user()` union-type category carried throughout this module since Feature #2 (more call sites in `Leads\Index.php`, not a new category); one genuinely new diagnostic (a `Collection` generics gap traced to `Tenant::tenantUsers()`'s own pre-existing missing-generics declaration) was fixed at the root — querying `TenantUser::where('tenant_id', ...)` directly instead of through that under-specified relation — not suppressed. No regressions confirmed against Lead Scoring, Lead Tagging, Deal/Opportunity, Tasks/Reminders, Bulk Actions, CSV Import, or Reporting & Analytics — every completed feature's own test files pass unmodified.

**Feature #9 — Workflow / Nurturing Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 14. Feature #10 — SLA / Response Tracking (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Scope — first-response-time measurement, live-computed, read-only reporting only**: no SLA target/deadline configuration, no breach detection, no breach persistence, no breach alerts, no new notification type, no scheduler, no queue job, no SLA history table, no new response-capture mechanism, no caching, no generic SLA engine. `Lead.first_contacted_at` is used exactly as it already behaves (set the first time a lead transitions away from the tenant's initial status, in `Leads\Show::updateStatus()` and its two bulk-action equivalents in `Leads\Index.php`) — this feature does not modify how or when that column is written.
- **Metrics — four, extending the existing Feature #8 reporting page**: Responded (count where `first_contacted_at IS NOT NULL`), Not Responded (`total leads in window - responded`, derived from the page's already-computed `volumeCount()` rather than a second `COUNT(*)` query), Average First Response Time, Median First Response Time — both durations in hours, `null` (never `0`) when the responded count is zero, rendered as "No data" in the UI. Daily first-response trend and response-time distribution/buckets were deliberately **not** built for Phase 1 — see deferred items below.
- **`App\Services\LeadReportingService::firstResponseSummary(Tenant, CarbonInterface, CarbonInterface)`** reuses the existing `baseQuery()` unchanged (explicit `tenant_id` filter, `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')`, `created_at` within the shared date range — identical scope to every other Feature #8 metric) with one added `whereNotNull('first_contacted_at')` filter, fetching only `created_at`/`first_contacted_at` via a single `get(['created_at', 'first_contacted_at'])` call. Average and median are computed **in PHP** from that one fetched result set (`Carbon::diffInSeconds()` per row, then mean/median over the array) — deliberately not `EXTRACT(EPOCH ...)`/`date_trunc`/`percentile_cont` in SQL, to stay fully portable between Postgres (production) and SQLite (tests) without a driver branch, unlike `dailyVolumeBreakdown()`'s existing date expression. Returns only `responded_count`/`average_hours`/`median_hours` — `not_responded_count` is deliberately computed by the caller (`LeadReports\Index`), not this service method, so the page's existing `volumeCount()` value is reused instead of issuing a redundant total-count query. Verified by a dedicated test asserting this method issues **exactly one query**.
- **Date window**: identical semantics to every other metric on this page (`created_at >= startOfDay(fromDate)`, `created_at < startOfDay(toDate + 1 day)`) — the window is based on **lead creation date, not response date**. A lead created inside the selected window whose response lands after the window closes is still counted, with its full actual response duration, never clipped to the window boundary — verified by a dedicated test.
- **Closed leads are not excluded** — a closed lead with `first_contacted_at = null` (e.g. a CSV-imported row created directly in a closed status, which never went through the "transition away from initial status" write) counts in Not Responded and is excluded from average/median, exactly like any other never-contacted lead; no separate closed-lead handling exists.
- **Merged-away leads are strictly excluded** via the same `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` already in `baseQuery()` — no pre-merge history from a loser lead is combined into the survivor's numbers (confirmed against `LeadMergeService`: date/lifecycle columns are never touched by a merge, so the survivor's own `first_contacted_at` is unaffected either way).
- **A reopened lead keeps its original response time** — `first_contacted_at` is never reset by this feature (nothing in this feature writes to that column at all); the existing `?? now()` idempotency guard in `Show::updateStatus()`/`Index.php`'s bulk equivalents is what already guarantees this in production, verified here by a regression test.
- **Authorization/tenant isolation — no new `PermissionSlug`, no new gate**: reuses `LeadReports\Index::mount()`'s existing `Gate::authorize('viewAny', Lead::class)` + `abort_unless(Auth::user()->isClientAdmin(), 403)` unchanged; tenant isolation is inherited entirely from `baseQuery()`'s explicit `tenant_id` filter, the same discipline as every other query in this module.
- **UI**: a new 4-tile `flux:card` stat row (Responded / Not Responded / Average First Response Time / Median First Response Time) on the existing `/leads/reports` page, positioned after the "Lead Volume" card and before the "Daily Lead Volume" table, plus a one-line explanatory note clarifying the creation-date-window semantics. "Not Responded" is styled amber (a "needs a look," not alarm-red, treatment, matching the Import History page's existing "Duplicate" stat color convention). No new route, no new sidebar entry — same page, same URL as Feature #8.
- **No new index** — this query's `WHERE` shape (`tenant_id` + `created_at` range) is identical to `volumeCount()`'s existing query and is already fully served by the `leads(tenant_id, created_at)` composite index added in Feature #8; `first_contacted_at` is only ever selected, never filtered/sorted at the database level, so it needs no index of its own. Confirmed correct by implementation — no migration was needed.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #10 Phase 1** (not started): SLA target/deadline configuration, breach detection, breach persistence, breach alerts/notifications, any new scheduled command or queue job, an SLA history table, assignment-response as a distinct metric, status-stage-duration reporting, daily first-response trend (would require a new grouped query and an undecided zero-fill semantic for an *average* metric — not a free byproduct of the existing daily-volume table's zero-fill technique), response-time distribution/buckets (would require inventing bucket-boundary definitions not specified by approved scope), Client User visibility into this block.

**Verification**: 14 new tests, all passing (`LeadResponseTimeReportingTest`) — responded/not-responded correctness, average correctness, median correctness (both odd- and even-count responded sets), `null`-never-`0` for an empty dataset (including a UI assertion that "0 hours"/"0.0 hours" never renders), merged-lead exclusion, tenant isolation, a directly-closed (CSV-import-style) lead counting as Not Responded and excluded from average, a reopened lead retaining its original response time, a response landing after the window boundary still using its full actual duration, Client User 403, and a query-count assertion proving `firstResponseSummary()` issues exactly one query. The existing `LeadReportingTest` (Feature #8) — 17/17 — was re-run unmodified and re-passed identically, with zero changes to that file. Full `tests/Feature/Leads` + `Notifications` + `Broadcasting`: 765/768 passed, same 2 pre-existing failures documented since Feature #2 (`LeadsIndexListenerTest`'s `'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated` mismatch — confirmed unrelated, since this feature never touches `Leads\Index.php` or its broadcast listeners), 1 pre-existing skip. Full application suite: 877/880 passed — a clean +14 over the Feature #9 baseline of 863/866, same 2 pre-existing failures, same 1 pre-existing skip, zero new failures. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 356 vs. the 352 baseline established at Feature #9's closure — the +4 traced individually to the exact same already-accepted categories already present throughout `LeadReports\Index.php` (Livewire `#[Computed]` magic-property access — one more occurrence each for `$this->rangeError` and the new `$this->volumeCount` reference inside `firstResponseSummary()` — and `Auth::user(): PlatformUser|TenantUser|null` union-type noise on `->tenant`) — no new error category, no suppressions, no `@phpstan-ignore` comments. Changed-file scope verified via `git status` to be exactly the 4 approved files (`LeadReportingService.php`, `LeadReports/Index.php`, `lead-reports.blade.php`, `LeadResponseTimeReportingTest.php`) — no Feature #1–#9 file, migration, route, or the two pre-existing alert commands (`SendLeadAlertNotifications`/`SendTaskAlertNotifications`) were touched.

**Feature #10 — SLA / Response Tracking Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 15. Feature #11 — Custom Fields (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Pure EAV, not a JSON/JSONB column on `leads`** — `App\Models\LeadCustomFieldDefinition` (table `lead_custom_field_definitions`) is a tenant-owned catalog entry, and `App\Models\LeadCustomFieldValue` (table `lead_custom_field_values`) is one row per (lead, definition) with a non-empty value. `leads` itself gained zero new columns. The JSON-blob alternative was explicitly evaluated and rejected during inspection (Postgres-vs-SQLite portability risk, and incompatibility with this module's existing per-field merge-conflict UI) — see the approved Architecture Proposal's Section D/E for the full comparison.
- **Tenant-owned catalog, not Platform-Admin-managed** — same shape as `LeadTag`/`LeadScoringRule`: `tenant_id` stored directly, no `BelongsToTenant` trait, every query filters `tenant_id` explicitly. `lead_custom_field_values` also carries `tenant_id` directly (same discipline as `LeadDuplicateLink`/`Deal`/`Task`), `unique(lead_id, lead_custom_field_definition_id)`, and the definition FK is `restrictOnDelete`.
- **Fixed Phase 1 type set** — `App\Enums\LeadCustomFieldType`: `text`, `number`, `date`, `select` only (no multi-select/checkbox). Each type owns its own `validationRules(required, options)`; `LeadCustomFieldDefinition::validationRules()` just applies a definition's own live `is_required`/`options` to its own type. This is the single source of truth both the manual-create form and the Lead detail/flyout edit form validate against.
- **Client Admin catalog CRUD** at `/leads/custom-fields` (route name `tenant-leads.custom-fields`, `App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadCustomFields\Index`), gated by a new `App\Policies\LeadCustomFieldDefinitionPolicy` that — like `LeadTagPolicy`/`LeadScoringRulePolicy` before it — reuses the existing `lead-management` `update` permission + `isClientAdmin()`. **No new `PermissionSlug`.** A sidebar entry ("Custom Fields") was added alongside the existing "Lead Sources"/"Reports & Analytics" items, following Feature #8's own precedent for adding a nav entry to a standalone Lead Management sub-page. Machine `key` is auto-slugged from `name` (`LeadTag`-style), tenant-scoped unique, with the same pre-check + `UniqueConstraintViolationException` race-catch every sibling catalog page uses.
- **Required-field semantics are explicitly non-retroactive**: `is_required` is only ever asserted inside a create/edit submission's own dynamically-built validation rules, assembled fresh from the tenant's currently-active definitions at submit time. No migration, cron, or lazy check ever walks existing leads to flag them invalid, and marking a field required later never blocks editing an unrelated field on a lead that has no value for it.
- **Type/options immutability** — once any `LeadCustomFieldValue` row exists for a definition, its `type` and `options` become permanently locked (blocked with a validation error in `LeadCustomFields\Index::save()`); `name`/`is_required`/`sort_order`/`is_active` stay freely editable regardless. There is no safe, defined reinterpretation of existing typed data across a type change, so this is blocked outright rather than silently transformed or discarded.
- **Field lifecycle**: deactivating (`is_active = false`) hides a field from future create/edit forms but leaves its already-captured values fully visible, read-only, on any lead that has one (`Show::customFieldDisplayRows()` unions active definitions with any inactive-but-still-valued one) — fully reversible via reactivation. Deleting a definition is **blocked outright while any value exists** (the `LeadStatus`/`Currency` "refuse, don't cascade" pattern, not `LeadTag`'s cascade pattern), with the delete-confirmation modal showing the exact usage count first; deleting an unused definition is a plain hard delete.
- **Lead create/edit integration**: a new `App\Actions\Tenant\SaveLeadCustomFieldValues` action is the single validate-against-current-active-definitions/diff/upsert-or-delete primitive shared by both `Leads\Index::saveLead()` (manual creation) and `Leads\Show::updateCustomFields()` (detail/flyout edit) — never duplicated logic across the two paths. A submitted definition id that doesn't belong to the current tenant, or that isn't currently active, is silently ignored, never written — the action re-derives the real definition set from the database rather than trusting the caller's keys. A blank submitted value **deletes** the value row rather than storing an empty string.
- **UI placement**: a new "Custom Fields" card (`partials/custom-fields-card.blade.php`) on both the full Lead detail page (`show.blade.php`) and the Kanban flyout (`show-panel.blade.php`), mirroring the Deals/Tasks/Notes cards' dual-surface inclusion exactly. **Deliberately absent from the Kanban card and the Leads table** — same "detail-page/flyout only, not card/table" precedent Feature #1's duplicate badge already established, avoiding an undefined "compact display of N arbitrary tenant-defined fields" UX.
- **Audit logging reuses `LeadDetailUpdateLog` as-is — no new history table.** `SaveLeadCustomFieldValues` returns the human-readable **names** (not raw definition ids/keys) of whatever fields actually changed; the caller writes exactly one log entry per save containing only those names, or none at all on a true no-op (diff-based, same discipline as `applyDetailChanges()`).
- **Merge integration (Feature #1) is a separate, bespoke step — custom field values are in neither `MERGEABLE_FIELDS` nor `CHILD_MODELS`**, and the existing scalar-field merge logic in `LeadMergeService` was not modified at all. Two new methods: `conflictingCustomFieldValues()` (namespaced `custom_field:{definitionId}` keys, mergeable into the same `$conflicts`/`field_resolutions` array `conflictingFields()` already populates, so `Show`'s existing merge modal needed zero template changes — only `mergeFieldLabel()`/`mergeFieldValueLabel()` gained a `custom_field:` branch to resolve a tenant-defined name live from the catalog) and `autoFillCustomFieldValues()`. Approved behavior, a deliberate and scoped divergence from `conflictingFields()`'s own scalar-field default: **survivor blank + loser has a value → auto-copied directly, never surfaced as a conflict; both have different non-blank values → explicit conflict in the existing modal, defaulting to keep-survivor, admin may choose loser.** Equal or both-blank values are a no-op. The loser's own value rows are never reparented (same reasoning as Tags' `unionTags()` needing its own bespoke step) — they simply remain attached to the retired, never-hard-deleted loser lead, fully queryable.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #11 Phase 1** (not started): CSV import support for custom fields, bulk custom-field editing, custom-field filtering/search/sorting, reporting/dashboard breakdowns, webhook custom-field ingestion, Website Form integration, multi-select/checkbox field types, field-level (per-field) permissions, retroactive backfill of existing leads, any historical value-change timeline beyond the single `LeadDetailUpdateLog` entry per save, and a generic dynamic-form engine.

**Verification**: 50 new tests, all passing across 5 files (`LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest`, `LeadCustomFieldValueTest`, `LeadCustomFieldEditTest`, `LeadCustomFieldDisplayTest`, `LeadCustomFieldMergeTest`) — catalog CRUD/tenant isolation/authorization/slug collision/type-immutability, all four field types' validation (valid/invalid/required/optional/inactive), manual-creation and detail-edit integration (diff-based single log entry, no-op produces none, cross-tenant id rejected), detail-page/flyout display plus absence from the Kanban card/Leads table, an N+1 query-count guard, and the full merge conflict/auto-fill/audit-snapshot behavior above. `tests/Feature/Leads/`: 761/762 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures (one full-suite run surfaced the already-documented, order-dependent `KanbanBoardTest` Faker-data collision — reproduced 0/3 in isolation, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated, not a Feature #11 regression). Full application suite: 927/930 passed — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2 (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`), plus the same 1 pre-existing skip — zero new failures. Existing Feature #1 and Feature #3–#10 test files (including every merge test) all re-ran green, unmodified. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 356 (Feature #10's closing baseline) → 375; every additional diagnostic traced individually to the same already-accepted categories already pervasive throughout this module (`Auth::user(): PlatformUser|TenantUser|null` union-type noise, Livewire `#[Computed]` magic-property access, and one already-pre-existing `nullsafe`-combined-with-`??` idiom identical in kind to an untouched line a few lines below it in `Leads\Show.php`) — no new category, no suppressions, no `@phpstan-ignore` comments. Two genuinely fixable issues found during this pass were fixed at the root rather than accepted: two redundant nullsafe-plus-null-coalesce compositions in `Show.php` (rewritten as explicit null checks) and one flaky new test relying on `Lead::factory()`'s default Faker phone number occasionally failing the existing phone-format validation (fixed by setting an explicit valid phone in that test).

**Feature #11 — Custom Fields Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 16. Feature #12 — Source Cost / ROI (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Cost per Lead only — no revenue/ROI in Phase 1.** `App\Models\LeadSourceCost` (table `lead_source_costs`) is a tenant-owned catalog entry, same shape as `LeadTag`/`LeadScoringRule`/`LeadCustomFieldDefinition`: direct `tenant_id`, no `BelongsToTenant`, every query filters `tenant_id` explicitly. One row = one source's recorded spend for one **calendar month** (`month` normalized to the 1st, `amount` decimal(12,2), `currency_id` FK to the shared `Currency` catalog), `unique(tenant_id, lead_source_id, month)`, `lead_source_id` `restrictOnDelete`. `leads`/`lead_sources` gained no new columns.
- **Deliberately separate from `LeadSourcePolicy`**: a new `App\Policies\LeadSourceCostPolicy` (Client-Admin-only, reuses the existing `lead-management` `update` permission — **no new `PermissionSlug`**) governs cost entries; `LeadSource` itself remains exactly as Platform-Admin-owned/tenant-read-only as before. Catalog CRUD lives at `/leads/source-costs` (`App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadSourceCosts\Index`), with a sidebar entry alongside Custom Fields/Reports and a contextual "Manage Source Costs" link from the Reports page. Manual and CsvImport source types are excluded from the eligible-source list, both in the UI and re-verified server-side against a tampered submission.
- **One consistent currency per source, enforced concurrency-safely**: a source's first cost entry may use any tenant-enabled currency; every entry after that must match it, rejected with a validation error otherwise. `LeadSourceCosts\Index::save()` runs the currency-consistency check, the month-uniqueness check, and the write inside one `DB::transaction()`, locking the **`LeadSource` row itself** (`lockForUpdate()`) before reading any existing cost rows — not a `LeadSourceCost` row, which may not exist yet for a source's very first entry. This is the same "lock a stable parent row" idiom `Lead::nextIdentifier()` already uses on `Tenant`, and it's what closes the race a first check-then-act implementation had: two concurrent first-time submissions for the same source can no longer both observe "no currency yet" and pick different ones.
- **Reporting**: `App\Services\LeadReportingService::sourceCostSummary()` extends the existing `/leads/reports` page (Feature #8/#10's shared date range) with a "Cost per Lead" card, positioned after "Source Performance." Calendar-month granularity only — **never prorated**. A month only partially covered by the selected range is excluded from calculation and named in an on-page disclaimer. A fully-covered month with **no** recorded cost entry for a source is treated as "not tracked," never as `$0` spend, and contributes to neither the cost sum nor the paired lead count for that source. `cost_per_lead` is `null` (not a divide-by-zero) when the contributing lead count is zero. Different sources' currencies are never blended or converted — each row keeps its own source's currency, and any tenant-wide total is subtotaled per currency. `volumeCount`, `dailyVolumeBreakdown`, `statusBreakdown`, `sourceBreakdown`, `assignmentBreakdown`, `firstResponseSummary` are all unchanged.
- **Two minimal, additive safeguards to pre-existing code, both required correctness fixes, not optional scope**: `Leads\Index::deleteSource()` gained one guard (`$source->costs()->exists()`) alongside its existing leads-check, so a source with recorded spend but zero leads can't be deleted out from under its cost history. `Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteCurrency()` gained one more in-use OR-clause (`LeadSourceCost::where('currency_id', ...)->exists()`), matching the exact precedent Feature #4 set for `Deal.currency_id`.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #12 Phase 1** (not started): true ROI / revenue attribution via `Deal.value`/`DealStatus::Won`; any currency conversion; arbitrary/custom-range or weekly cost entries; proration of any kind; campaign-level (sub-source) cost tracking; historical cost trend charts; CSV import/export of cost data; any further change to `LeadSourcePolicy`/`LeadSource` beyond the one relation; automated cost ingestion from ad platforms; filtering/searching/sorting leads by cost or cost-per-lead.

**Verification**: 21 new tests across 2 files (`LeadSourceCostCatalogTest`, `LeadSourceCostReportingTest`) covering catalog CRUD/tenant isolation/authorization, Manual/CsvImport exclusion (UI list + server-side rejection even if tampered), duplicate-month rejection, currency-consistency enforcement on both create and edit, the new `deleteSource()`/`deleteCurrency()` guards, and the full reporting semantics above (fully-covered-vs-partial-month classification, untracked-month exclusion, multi-currency unblended display with correct subtotals, an N+1 query-count guard). A follow-up verification pass found one real, narrow concurrency gap in the currency-consistency check (a check-then-act race between two first-time submissions for the same brand-new source) — fixed by wrapping the check and write in a `DB::transaction()` locked on the `LeadSource` row, with 2 additional regression tests (14 total in `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest`) proving the lock targets the correct row even when the source has zero existing cost history, and that two sequential first-time saves for a brand-new source correctly enforce the invariant end-to-end. Full application suite: 950/953 passed — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatch documented since Feature #2, plus the same 1 pre-existing skip — zero new failures, confirmed stable across repeated runs. Existing Feature #1–#11 test files (including `LeadReportingTest`, `LeadResponseTimeReportingTest`, `LeadManagementSettingsTest`, `DealManagementTest`, and Lead-source-deletion coverage in `FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest`) all re-ran green, unmodified. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 375 (Feature #11's closing baseline) → 396; every additional diagnostic traced individually to the same already-accepted categories pervasive throughout this module (`Auth::user(): PlatformUser|TenantUser|null` union-type noise, Livewire `#[Computed]` magic-property access, and the same self-referential narrowed-return-type quirk already present at `LeadReportingService::assignmentBreakdown()`) — no new category, no suppressions.

**Feature #12 — Source Cost / ROI Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 17. Feature #13 — Website Form Builder (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Multi-form from the start — no single-form-per-tenant assumption.** `App\Models\WebsiteForm` (table `website_forms`) is a tenant-owned catalog entry, same shape as `LeadTag`/`LeadScoringRule`/`LeadCustomFieldDefinition`/`LeadSourceCost`: direct `tenant_id`, no `BelongsToTenant`, every query filters `tenant_id` explicitly. A tenant may create any number of forms.
- **One shared, lazily-created `LeadSource` per tenant** (`type = WebsiteForm`) backs every `WebsiteForm` a tenant creates — `WebsiteForms\Index::save()` does a `firstOrCreate()` on first form creation and reuses that same row (including a tenant's pre-existing legacy Website Form source, if one already exists) for every subsequent form. Per-form reporting is explicitly deferred to a future phase; `website_form_id`/`website_form_name` are captured in each Lead's `source_data` snapshot now so that future reporting doesn't need a schema change.
- **Form-specific fields are a separate table, not a JSON column** — `App\Models\WebsiteFormField` (table `website_form_fields`) is one row per field a specific form accepts: either a fixed Lead column (`fixed_field`, one of `email`/`phone`/`message` — `name` is always implicit and required, never has its own row) or a reference to an existing `LeadCustomFieldDefinition`. `restrictOnDelete` on the custom-field FK, `cascadeOnDelete` on the parent-form FK (a field-configuration row is not data, so it's safe to cascade when its form is deleted). Two unique constraints prevent attaching the same fixed field or the same custom field twice to one form.
- **Per-form high-entropy token, hashed at rest** — `Str::random(40)` (the same convention Sanctum's own personal access tokens use), SHA-256 hashed into `token_hash` (unique). The plaintext is shown to the admin exactly once, in a dedicated modal, at creation time and again on regeneration — never persisted, never re-displayable afterward. Regeneration is a plain hash replacement with no grace period: the old token stops working immediately, proven end-to-end by a real two-request test (old token 404s, new token works, right after regenerating).
- **New public submission endpoint, separate from and non-disruptive to the legacy webhook** — `POST /api/webhook/website-form/{tenant_code}/{form_token}` (`LeadWebhookController::websiteFormSubmit()`), authenticated purely by the token (resolved via `resolveWebsiteForm()`, which independently checks tenant-active, form-active, and tenant-ownership, all folding into an identical 404 so a probing request learns nothing about which check failed). The pre-existing `POST /api/webhook/website-form/{tenant_code}` (`websiteForm()`, API-key-authenticated) was not modified in any way — confirmed by direct code diff, not just by its tests still passing. `createLead()` (the one shared "create Lead + fire `LeadReceived`" helper used by all 5 pre-existing ingestion channels) gained one new optional parameter, default `[]`, so every existing call site's behavior is provably unchanged.
- **The server derives everything; the client controls nothing** — `App\Actions\Tenant\ValidateWebsiteFormSubmission` builds its validation rules and accepted-field set entirely from the token-resolved `WebsiteForm`'s own `WebsiteFormField` rows, never from the POST body's shape. A submitted key that isn't part of that specific form's own configuration (including another of the same tenant's forms' fields) is silently discarded, not stored, not erroring — a deliberate divergence from the legacy endpoint's "keep every extra field" behavior. Custom field values are additionally re-validated a second time by the unmodified, pre-existing `SaveLeadCustomFieldValues` action, which independently re-derives the tenant's own active definitions rather than trusting any caller's keys — two independent layers, neither trusting client input.
- **Existing Custom Field system (Feature #11) is reused as-is, not duplicated.** A form can expose any of the tenant's own custom field definitions; a definition later deactivated is silently skipped in a submission (not a hard failure) without blocking the rest of the fields; a definition still referenced by any `WebsiteFormField` cannot be deleted (`LeadCustomFieldDefinition::isReferencedByWebsiteForm()`, a new additive guard in the pre-existing `LeadCustomFields\Index::delete()`, mirroring the exact `hasValues()` precedent already there) — no orphaned field configuration is reachable.
- **Honeypot, no CAPTCHA** — a fixed hidden field (`_gotcha`); a non-empty value returns the identical `200 {"status":"ok"}` a real success would (never tipping off a bot) but creates nothing. CAPTCHA was explicitly excluded from Phase 1 scope.
- **Two-tier rate limiting — the first rate limiting on any public webhook route in this codebase.** `AppServiceProvider::configureRateLimiting()` registers `website-form-per-form` (20/min, keyed by token+IP) and `website-form-per-tenant` (60/min, keyed by tenant_code+IP), applied to the new route as **two separate** `throttle:` middleware entries — a real, non-obvious Laravel trap was hit and fixed here: `throttle:name1,name2` (comma-joined in a single middleware string) does **not** register two named limiters, it's parsed as two positional `$maxAttempts`/`$decayMinutes` arguments instead, silently breaking with a `MissingRateLimiterException` at request time despite both limiters being correctly registered. The legacy endpoint deliberately received no new rate limiting.
- **No hosted forms, no public form page, no iframe, no JS embed SDK.** This app never renders or serves the form itself — the tenant's own developer builds the HTML and POSTs to the form's endpoint. The one UI addition beyond plain CRUD is a read-only "Integration Details" panel (endpoint URL, accepted field keys, which are required, an example JSON payload) — documentation only, not a hosted schema/public endpoint.
- **Authorization — no new `PermissionSlug`.** `App\Policies\WebsiteFormPolicy` reuses the existing `lead-management` `update` permission + `isClientAdmin()`, the identical bar `LeadTagPolicy`/`LeadScoringRulePolicy`/`LeadCustomFieldDefinitionPolicy`/`LeadSourceCostPolicy` already use. The public submission endpoint correctly carries no authenticated-authorization check at all — the token is its sole credential, by design.
- **Admin UI**: `/leads/website-forms` (`App\Livewire\Tenant\WebsiteForms\Index`), full CRUD (create/edit/activate-deactivate/delete/configure-fields/regenerate-token) plus the Integration Details panel, following the same Livewire catalog pattern as every sibling page. Deleting a form is unconditionally allowed — a `WebsiteForm` has no hard FK from `Lead` (attribution is a `source_data` snapshot only), so deletion never touches any existing Lead, proven by a dedicated test.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #13 Phase 1** (not started): hosted/public forms, iframe embedding, a JS embed SDK, a drag-and-drop field builder, conditional field logic, multi-step forms, file uploads, CAPTCHA, advanced spam scoring beyond the basic honeypot, per-form analytics/conversion reporting, A/B testing, multi-language forms, campaign-specific forms, email automation, advanced workflow automation, webhook delivery retries, advanced styling/theming, and any per-domain allowlisting on the new builder endpoint (the token is the primary security boundary in Phase 1).

**Verification**: 24 new tests across 2 files (`WebsiteFormSubmissionTest` — 15, `WebsiteFormCatalogTest` — 9) covering: multiple forms per tenant sharing one source; full multi-tenant/multi-form token isolation in both directions; invalid-token and inactive-form 404s; cross-form field-key leakage discarded; unconfigured fixed fields never stored; required/wrong-type/deactivated custom field handling; honeypot; `LeadReceived` firing exactly once; legacy-and-builder coexistence sharing one source; token-regeneration invalidating the old token end-to-end; form deletion leaving historical Leads untouched; the custom-field delete guard from both the submission side and the admin-delete side; authorization denial for a Client User without the `update` permission; token-hash-only-at-rest; and — added during the dedicated Final Verification Pass below, after it was identified as the one genuinely critical gap in the initial test set — the per-tenant rate limiter's own core guarantee (abuse spread across several forms, each individually staying under its own per-form cap, still trips the tenant-wide ceiling). Full application suite: 974/977 passed — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2 (confirmed via `git diff`/`git log` to predate this feature and touch a file this feature never modifies), plus the same 1 pre-existing skip — zero new failures. Existing legacy website-form webhook tests, Feature #11's Custom Field tests, and every other completed feature's own test files all re-ran green, unmodified. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 396 (Feature #12's closing baseline) → 414 project-wide, but **zero** of those errors are in any file this feature added or touched — reconfirmed with a cleared result-cache full run after an initial diagnostic-tooling false alarm during this feature's own audit process; the +18 is fully attributable to other already-untracked, out-of-scope files already sitting in the working tree before this feature began.

**A dedicated Final Verification Pass (code-level audit against the approved architecture, run after the initial implementation) found and closed one real gap before closure**: the initial test set fully proved the per-form (20/min) rate limiter but never independently exercised the per-tenant (60/min) limiter's own reason for existing — catching an attacker who spreads requests across several of a tenant's forms, each individually staying under its own per-form cap. One focused regression test was added and passed, confirming the tenant-wide ceiling works correctly and independently of the per-form one. No application code required a change as a result of this pass — only the test suite gained the missing coverage.

**Known non-blocking / pre-existing findings, explicitly not fixed as part of this feature** (see the Final Verification Pass for full detail):
- A `tenant_code`-enumeration oracle: the new endpoint's 404 responses distinguish "tenant_code doesn't exist" from "tenant_code exists" via differing exception messages. This is **inherited from the pre-existing `resolveSource()` pattern** already used by every other public webhook route (Facebook, WhatsApp, Instagram, the legacy website-form endpoint) — not newly introduced by this feature. A future cross-cutting fix (a uniform, non-model-revealing 404 body across every webhook resolver) is recommended but out of this feature's scope.
- UI verification was performed at the Livewire-component level only, not in an actual browser session (checkbox-group bindings, modals, the copy-to-clipboard token field) — disclosed rather than silently assumed.
- `select`/`date`-type custom fields and a raw-extra-Lead-column-key rejection aren't separately exercised at the public-submission-test level (both are covered by code review and by Feature #11's own existing test coverage of the shared validation logic) — minor coverage gaps, not defects.
- The 2 pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and the pre-existing PHPStan baseline noise, both already documented above and unrelated to this feature.

**Feature #13 — Website Form Builder Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 18. Feature #14 — Consent / GDPR (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Dedicated, structured consent architecture — not Lead columns, not a Custom Field.** Two new tables: `lead_consents` holds the CURRENT effective state, one row per `(lead_id, consent_type)` (`unique` constraint), upserted on every grant/withdraw; `lead_consent_events` is the permanent, append-only history — one row per action, never updated or deleted by any code path in this app (same "immutable in practice" convention `LeadStatusLog`/`LeadScoreLog` already follow). Both tables carry `tenant_id` directly, `cascadeOnDelete` on `lead_id`, no `BelongsToTenant` reliance.
- **Two independently tracked consent types, `App\Enums\ConsentType`** (`Privacy`/Data-Processing, `Marketing`/Communication) — a plain, engineering-controlled PHP enum (matching `DealStatus`/`TaskStatus`/`WebsiteFormFieldType`'s precedent), not a tenant-configurable catalog. Terms Acceptance was explicitly **not** added as a third type in Phase 1.
- **Status model — `Granted`/`Withdrawn` only; "Not Provided" is the absence of a row**, never a third status value — the same "no row and blank are the same state" discipline `LeadCustomFieldValue` already established.
- **`App\Actions\Tenant\RecordLeadConsent`** is the single grant/withdraw primitive every caller goes through — one `DB::transaction()` per call that upserts the current-state row and appends exactly one history event together, never partially.
- **Website Form Builder integration**: a new `WebsiteFormFieldType::Consent` case (alongside Fixed/Custom), with `consent_type`/`is_required`/`policy_version`/`policy_url` columns on `website_form_fields`, configured per form in `WebsiteForms\Index`'s existing modal. `App\Actions\Tenant\ValidateWebsiteFormSubmission` derives every consent field's type/required-ness/policy metadata entirely from that form's own stored configuration — never from the request. Checkbox semantics: optional + omitted/false → no record (stays Not Provided); required + omitted/false → 422; a present, non-boolean-ish value → 422; an explicit true → a grant, recorded via `RecordLeadConsent` immediately after `createLead()` in `LeadWebhookController::websiteFormSubmit()`, snapshotting the same `website_form_id`/`website_form_name` already used for `source_data`, plus `ip_address`/`user_agent`. An unconfigured or cross-form consent key is silently discarded, exactly like every other Feature #13 field type.
- **Manual Lead management**: a new "Consent" card on the Lead detail page and Kanban flyout (`App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::grantConsent()`/`withdrawConsent()`) shows both types' current status, last-changed timestamp/source, and inline history. **No new `PermissionSlug`, no dedicated `LeadConsentPolicy`** — both actions reuse the existing `Gate::allows('update', $lead)` bar, the same lightweight authorization every other lead-child action (notes, tags, custom fields) already uses; withdrawal deliberately is **not** gated behind the stricter `delete` permission.
- **Audit/activity integration**: `Leads\Show::activity()` gained a `consent_change` branch sourced from `LeadConsentEvent`, rendered in `activity-card.blade.php` alongside every other event type — manual events attribute the acting `TenantUser`; Website Form and merge-synthetic events correctly show no user, with the form name or "Lead Merge" shown instead.
- **Lead merge — conservative, Withdrawn always wins, nothing ever lost**: `LeadConsentEvent` (history) is a plain `CHILD_MODELS` reparent (both leads' full histories preserved on the survivor, no dedup needed — it's append-only). `LeadConsent` (current state) gets bespoke handling in `LeadMergeService::mergeConsents()`, since its unique constraint rules out a blind reparent: either side `Withdrawn` → survivor ends `Withdrawn` (never silently downgraded back to `Granted`); both `Granted` → stays `Granted`; only one side has a record → copied onto the survivor; neither side has one → stays Not Provided. A synthetic `source = Merge` event is written for every type where at least one side had a record, even when nothing changed, documenting the merge outcome.
- **Legacy Website Form webhook (`POST /api/webhook/website-form/{tenant_code}`) is completely unchanged** — confirmed by direct code diff, not just by its existing tests re-passing unmodified. No reinterpretation of arbitrary `source_data` as consent was built.
- **External platform consent normalization explicitly deferred** — Facebook Lead Ads, Facebook Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp, and LinkedIn Lead Gen were not modified; any platform-native consent/opt-in field they carry remains unread, exactly as before this feature. Only the Website Form Builder and manual Lead management produce consent records in Phase 1.
- **Retention, anonymization, DSR workflows, a public privacy portal, a data-export/request portal, a DPO workflow, and any communication-enforcement engine were all explicitly evaluated during the Architecture Brief and deferred** — no strong architectural reason was found to pull any of them into Phase 1. The existing hard-delete-with-cascade-and-attachment-cleanup behavior (`LeadObserver`) is unchanged; consent management here is purely additive record-keeping, orthogonal to deletion/retention.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #14 Phase 1** (not started): Terms Acceptance as a third consent type, automatic retention enforcement, automatic deletion, automatic anonymization, a full GDPR data-subject-request workflow, a public privacy portal, a data export/request portal, a DPO workflow, a communication-enforcement engine (no outbound-to-lead communication exists in this app at all yet — see below), full Facebook/Instagram/WhatsApp/LinkedIn consent normalization, CAPTCHA, advanced legal-document management, and consent analytics/reporting beyond the basic audit/history already built.

**Verification**: 38 new tests across 3 files (`LeadConsentTest` — model/DB constraints, cascade, tenant isolation, lifecycle, and 8 merge-scenario tests; `WebsiteFormConsentSubmissionTest` — grant/optional-omit/required-omit/required-false/invalid-value/forged-field/cross-form-isolation/policy-snapshot/IP-UA-capture/honeypot/lenient-boolean; `LeadConsentManagementTest` — manual grant/withdraw, authorization positive and negative, card display, activity attribution for both Manual and Website Form sources, history ordering, event immutability), all passing. The Feature #14 + Feature #13 + legacy webhook + Custom Field + merge + deletion regression bundle: 134/134 passed. Full application suite: 1015 tests, 1012 passed, 2 failed, 1 skipped — the same 2 `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` failures documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2, zero new failures. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 421 vs. the 414 baseline established at Feature #13's closure — **zero** of the 421 errors are in any file this feature added or touched (verified with a cleared result cache after Feature #13's own audit uncovered a cache-corruption false-alarm risk); the +7 is attributable to other already-untracked, unrelated work already sitting in the working tree, the same pattern documented at every prior feature's closure. No regression was introduced into any completed Feature #1–#13 behavior.

**Feature #14 final verification status: READY FOR CLOSURE**, confirmed by the user.

**Known limitations carried forward as technical debt, not fixed as part of this feature:**
1. Marketing consent currently has no outbound-communication consumer — this app has no mechanism to send email/SMS/WhatsApp to a Lead at all yet, so recorded consent is forward-looking data only, pending Feature #15.
2. The pre-existing `tenant_code`-enumeration oracle on public webhook resolvers (documented at Feature #13's closure) remains unchanged — `resolveWebsiteForm()` inherits it, this feature adds no new instance of it.
3. Verification was performed at the Livewire-component level only; no browser-level UI session was run for the new Consent card's interactions or the admin form's consent-field toggles.
4. External-platform consent normalization (Facebook/Instagram/WhatsApp/LinkedIn) remains deferred — see the External platform bullet above.

**Feature #14 — Consent / GDPR Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 19. Feature #15 — In-App Communication (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Normalized `conversations` + `messages` tables, no `message_status_events` table.** `conversations` — one row per `(tenant_id, lead_source_id, channel, external_thread_id)`, `channel` ∈ {`whatsapp`, `messenger`, `instagram`} (a small, engineering-controlled enum, not a reuse of `LeadSourceType`), `external_thread_id` is the platform's own opaque thread identifier (WhatsApp: phone number; Messenger: PSID; Instagram: IGSID — deliberately never assumed to share a shape). `messages` — one row per inbound or outbound message, `unique(tenant_id, channel, external_message_id)` (nullable-safe), `status`/`status_updated_at`/`failure_reason` meaningful only for `direction = outbound`.
- **Additive inbound dual-write, no historical backfill.** `App\Actions\Tenant\RecordInboundMessage` runs alongside — never instead of — the existing `Lead.source_data` JSON append in all three inbound webhook handlers (`LeadWebhookController::facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()`, both the new-lead and follow-up-append paths). The legacy JSON write is byte-for-byte unchanged; confirmed by every pre-existing webhook test passing unmodified. `Lead::messageTimeline()` was extended, not rewritten, to also surface normalized rows.
- **`Lead::messageTimeline()` backward-compatibility fix (found and corrected during Final Verification, before closure — not a pre-existing bug, a defect in this feature's own first implementation).** An initial version switched to normalized rows exclusively the moment ANY existed for a lead, which silently hid a lead's entire pre-existing legacy message history the instant it received its first post-cutover inbound message — reproduced directly during audit (a lead with 2 legacy messages showed only 1 entry after one new inbound message arrived). Corrected to a per-*entry* boundary: a legacy JSON entry is shown only if it is strictly older than this lead's own first dual-written inbound message; every normalized row (inbound after cutover, plus every outbound reply) is always shown. A lead with no dual-written messages yet keeps its full legacy timeline untouched. Two regression tests added reproducing the exact failure and proving outbound messages render correctly alongside legacy history.
- **Outbound sending — WhatsApp, Messenger, Instagram, free-form only.** `App\Contracts\MessageSenderInterface` + `WhatsAppSender`/`MessengerSender`/`InstagramSender`, resolved via a plain `match()` factory (`MessageSenderFactory`), matching this module's existing "one enum, one match() per case" convention rather than a container-tag abstraction with no precedent here. Credentials are read fresh from `$conversation->leadSource->config` at send time, every time — never cached, never placed in a queued job payload (verified: `SendOutboundMessage`/`ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus` use `Illuminate\Foundation\Queue\Queueable`, which includes `SerializesModels` — jobs carry only a model reference, re-fetched from the database on execution). Recipient and channel are always server-derived from the resolved `Conversation`, never trusted from client input. `InstagramSender` reuses the exact existing `auth_mode`-based host branching (`graph.instagram.com` vs `graph.facebook.com`) `LeadWebhookController::fetchInstagramProfile()` already established — verified against the actual code, not assumed, and proven by a dedicated test per branch. No templates, no Human Agent tag, no Message Tags, no business-initiated/proactive messaging — a reply within the standard messaging window only.
- **`App\Services\OutboundEligibilityService`** is the single gate every send goes through, evaluated both at compose-time (UI state) and again, redundantly, inside the queued send job itself: lead not merged away; Privacy consent (Feature #14) not `Withdrawn` (blocks ALL outbound, not just marketing — a deliberate conservative safety rail); source active with required credentials present; the channel's own 24-hour messaging window (`MessageChannel::windowHours()`, a named per-channel constant, verified independently per platform — Messenger's general Message Tags feature was in fact deprecated by Meta globally as of February 2026, leaving even less post-window capability than originally scoped, reinforcing that "within-window reply only" is the correct Phase 1 boundary) still open. **Marketing consent is deliberately NOT checked** — a direct within-window reply to a customer-initiated conversation is service/transactional, not marketing; proven by a dedicated test that a withdrawn Marketing consent alone never blocks a service reply.
- **Queued outbound sending with same-row updates.** `Show::sendMessage()`/`resendMessage()` create a `status = pending` `Message` row synchronously, then dispatch `SendOutboundMessage` (`tries = 3`, backoff `[5, 15, 30]`s) — the job only ever updates that one row, never creates a second, making retry safe. A transient infrastructure/network failure may retry at the job level; a confirmed platform rejection (`SendResult::failure()`) marks the message `failed` immediately with no further attempt — manual resend only, always a brand-new `Message` row, the original failed row left untouched as a permanent record. A short `Cache::lock` (mirroring `LeadWebhookController::withDedupeLock()`'s existing idiom), keyed by conversation + a hash of the exact body, blocks a double-click/duplicate submission.
- **Status reconciliation is additive to the existing webhooks, always ACKs 200, and is channel-specific.** New `foreach` branches inside the existing `facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()` methods (never a new route) dispatch `App\Jobs\ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus` rather than updating inline — preserving the pre-existing "always acknowledge 200" policy those methods already documented. WhatsApp correlates reliably by `external_message_id` (wamid); Messenger/Instagram correlate primarily by conversation + watermark timestamp (Meta's own delivery/read webhook's `mids` array is documented as sometimes absent — verified via live documentation research, not assumed), using `mids` opportunistically when present. Status updates are strictly monotonic (`MessageStatus::rank()`) and never touch an inbound row.
- **Concurrency fix (found and corrected during Final Verification, before closure).** `ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus::applyStatus()` originally read an already-loaded `Message` and wrote via a plain `->update()` with no lock — two genuinely concurrent status webhooks for the same message (e.g. "delivered" immediately followed by "read", processed by two queue workers) could both read the same pre-update status and, depending on write order, downgrade the row. Fixed by re-fetching the row inside `DB::transaction()` with `lockForUpdate()`, making the read-check-write atomic per message — the same locking idiom Feature #12's `LeadSourceCost` concurrency fix already established for this codebase.
- **A not-yet-found WhatsApp status lookup retries via the job queue rather than being silently dropped** — the target `Message` row is always created before any send attempt, so a genuine "not found" almost always means a narrow timing race, not a permanently missing message; only after all retry attempts are exhausted is an operator-visible warning logged.
- **Lead-detail/Kanban-flyout composer only — no unified inbox.** One new card (`message-composer-card.blade.php`), visible only for a WhatsApp/Messenger/Instagram-sourced lead, gated by the existing `LeadPolicy::update()` (no new `PermissionSlug`, no dedicated policy class), showing live eligibility state, a text-only composer, and outbound message history with status badges and manual resend on a failed message.
- **Reuses the existing `LeadUpdated`/Reverb infrastructure exactly** — every outbound state change (created, sent, failed, delivered, read) fires the same pre-existing `LeadUpdated` event on the same pre-existing `tenant.{id}`/`lead.{id}` private channels; no new broadcast event or channel was created.
- **Rate limiting — approved numbers, adapted mechanism.** 30 outbound sends/user/minute and 100/tenant/minute, enforced via direct `RateLimiter::tooManyAttempts()`/`hit()` calls inside `Leads\Show::passesOutboundRateLimits()` (called from both `sendMessage()` and `resendMessage()`, before any `Message` row is created) rather than through `AppServiceProvider`-registered `RateLimiter::for()` + route `throttle:` middleware — an accepted, explicitly-approved architectural variation, since a Livewire component action has no natural attachment point for route-level middleware the way Feature #13's public webhook endpoint did. Both limits use the same underlying `Illuminate\Cache\RateLimiter`, both keys are scoped to already-authenticated/authorized context (never client input), and both methods are covered consistently.
- **Tenant isolation / security**: every new query chain terminates at an already-tenant-verified parent — `Conversation`/`Message` access always flows through `$this->lead`'s own relations (itself gated by `LeadPolicy::view()`/`update()` at `Show::mount()`), never a bare model lookup; `ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus` filters by `tenant_id` first in every query; credentials are never logged, never serialized into a queue payload, and always re-read fresh from `LeadSource.config` at send time. `MetaPermissionsController::REQUIRED_SCOPES` gained `whatsapp_business_messaging` alongside the existing `pages_messaging`/`instagram_manage_messages`, verified via live Meta documentation research to be the correct, checkable scope for WhatsApp Cloud API sending (an earlier assumption that WhatsApp had no checkable scope was corrected during the Final Architecture Validation pass, before implementation).

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #15 Phase 1** (not started): WhatsApp/Messenger/Instagram template messages and template management, Messenger's `HUMAN_AGENT`/Message Tags extended-window messaging (and Instagram's equivalent), business-initiated/proactive/marketing messaging (and the `ConsentType::Marketing` enforcement it would require), a unified cross-lead inbox, campaign/bulk/broadcast messaging, marketing automation, scheduled campaigns, a media/file/attachment composer for outbound (inbound attachment handling is untouched), voice/video calling, AI auto-replies/chatbot behavior, A/B testing, analytics dashboards for messaging, advanced conversation assignment/routing, a customer-facing portal, email channels, automatic retry of a confirmed platform send failure, live per-status-transition broadcast granularity beyond the existing full-lead `LeadUpdated` refresh, and any live pre-send Meta scope-validity check (scope readiness is checkable via the existing, now three-scope, `MetaPermissionsController` tool, but is not automated or surfaced in the compose UI itself).

**Verification**: focused Feature #15 suite (`InboundMessageDualWriteTest`, `OutboundMessageStatusReconciliationTest`, `OutboundEligibilityServiceTest`, `SendOutboundMessageTest`, `LeadMessageComposerTest`, plus one new `MetaPermissionsControllerTest` case) — 42/42 passed. Legacy webhook + Custom Field + merge + deletion + consent + Website Form regression bundle — 140/140 passed. Full application suite: 1053 tests, 1050 passed, 2 failed, 1 skipped — the same `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` failures documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2 (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`; confirmed via `git diff`/`git log` to predate this feature and touch a file Feature #15 never modifies), plus the same 1 pre-existing skip — zero new failures, verified stable across repeated full-suite runs both before and after the two Final-Verification fixes below. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 414 (Feature #14's closing baseline) → 428 project-wide at the time of closure, cleared result cache, re-verified stable across two consecutive runs — one genuinely fixable issue in this feature's own new code (`RecordInboundMessage::handle()`'s `$metadata` parameter missing a generic array-value type) was found and fixed at the root; after that fix, zero of the 428 errors were in any file this feature added or touched. A second, same-category issue was found during pre-commit validation for the end-of-day handoff on 2026-09-12 (see below) and fixed the same way, bringing the current count to **425**, still zero attributable to Feature #15.

**A dedicated Final Verification / Pre-Closure Audit (run after the initial implementation, before this closure) found and fixed two real issues, both narrowly scoped and directly required for correctness, neither a scope change:**
1. **`Lead::messageTimeline()` historical-visibility bug** — see above. Reproduced directly, fixed, re-verified with two new regression tests plus the full existing dual-write suite.
2. **`ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus` concurrent-write monotonicity race** — see above. Fixed with a transactional row lock, re-verified against all 7 reconciliation tests; true concurrent execution isn't independently testable under SQLite's single-connection test driver (the same documented limitation this codebase already accepts for Feature #12's own `LeadSourceCost` lock), verified instead by code review and the correctness of the locking construct itself.

Both fixes were applied and the full regression suite (1053 tests) was re-run clean after each, before this closure was approved.

**Post-closure note (2026-09-12, end-of-day handoff commit prep):** pre-commit PHPStan validation (cleared result cache) found a third, same-category, genuinely fixable issue that the original Final Verification Audit missed: `App\Support\SendResult`'s `$rawResponse` parameter (in `__construct()`, `success()`, and `failure()`) was typed `?array` with no value-type PHPDoc, producing 3 `missingType.iterableValue` errors. Fixed at the root with `@param array<string, mixed>|null $rawResponse` on all three — a docblock-only change, no behavior change, no new abstraction. PHPStan dropped from 428 to 425 (zero Feature #15-attributable, confirmed by path across two cleared-cache runs); the full regression suite (1053 tests, 1050 passed, same 2 pre-existing failures, same 1 pre-existing skip) and Pint were both re-verified clean after this fix. Feature #15 was not reopened — this was a narrowly-scoped correctness fix required to make this closure record accurate, applied under the same "real issue found during verification" allowance already used for the two fixes above.

**Known limitations / residual risks carried forward as documented technical debt, not fixed as part of this feature, and not treated as blockers:**
1. **Timeout-after-platform-acceptance ambiguity** — a response-read timeout occurring after a platform (WhatsApp/Messenger/Instagram) already accepted an outbound message is indistinguishable, at the HTTP-client level, from one that occurred before acceptance. No idempotency key is exposed by any of these platforms' send endpoints to close this fully; mitigated only by a low job retry count (`tries = 3`), not eliminated. A genuine, accepted platform-level limitation, not a defect in this implementation.
2. **Per-tenant Meta permission readiness is not automatically verified.** Whether a specific tenant's already-issued token actually carries `whatsapp_business_messaging`/`pages_messaging`/`instagram_manage_messages` can only be confirmed by running the existing `MetaPermissionsController` check for that tenant — this is not automated, scheduled, or surfaced inside the compose UI itself in Phase 1.
3. **Instagram's delivery/read-receipt reliability is platform-documented as weaker than Messenger's** — the UI never treats a missing read receipt as an error, per the approved design, but a true "read" confirmation may simply never arrive for some Instagram conversations; this is a Meta platform characteristic, not a gap in this implementation.
4. **No real browser/manual QA pass has been performed.** All UI verification in this feature was performed at the Livewire full-component-render level (which does render actual Blade/Flux markup and does exercise conditional visibility, `wire:click`/`wire:submit` bindings, and validation — materially stronger than a pure PHP unit test, but not a real browser session exercising JavaScript/Alpine/CSS). A real browser pass before wider rollout is recommended, consistent with how every UI-bearing feature in this module has been handled.
5. **A narrow, payload-malformation-only edge case in `messageTimeline()`'s legacy/normalized boundary**: if an inbound webhook payload is missing its own timestamp field entirely (never observed in a real Meta payload — confirmed via this codebase's own existing timestamp-handling code, which always expects one), the fallback timestamp used for that legacy entry could in principle collide down to the same second as this lead's cutover point. Low-probability, degenerate-payload-only; not a realistic production path.

**Feature #15 — In-App Communication Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 20. Known Technical Debt / Risks

- PHPStan baseline cleanup (the pre-existing `Auth::user()` union-type gap, the pre-existing `Lead.php` return-type gaps, etc.) is **separate technical debt**, not something to fix incidentally while building a feature.
- The Scoring Rules UI's numeric value input is free text with basic `is_numeric` tolerance — a tenant typing e.g. `"1,000"` doesn't error, it just never matches numerically. A UX rough edge, not a correctness bug.
- Time-derived scores (`days_since_created`, `days_since_last_status_change`) only refresh via the daily sweep (or an unrelated status/detail edit that happens to also trigger a recompute) — by design, not a bug.
- Feature #2 Phase 2b (badge/filter/sort/dashboard/etc., see §5) remains intentionally deferred.
- Feature #1's two backlog items (duplicate-detection index performance, merge budget/currency pairing — see §4) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #3's explicitly deferred items (multi-tag AND/OR filtering, tag-based scoring, tag analytics/reporting, bulk tag actions, CSV import/export for tags, inline tag creation — see §7) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #4's explicitly deferred items (Deal Kanban, Deal list route, reporting/forecasting, bulk Deal actions, automation, Deal-specific tags/notes/attachments, commissions, line items, invoicing, CRM integrations, configurable Deal sub-stages, data backfill — see §8) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #5's explicitly deferred items (recurring tasks, subtasks/dependencies, task comments/attachments, Task-specific tags, global Task dashboard/route, Task Kanban, bulk Task actions, email/SMS reminders, per-tenant timezone handling, Task-based scoring/reporting, follow-up/meeting field migration — see §9) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #6's explicitly deferred items (background/queued bulk processing, a bulk-actions dashboard, bulk Deal/Task actions, an aggregate bulk activity record, bulk search/filter redesign, "select all matching filtered results," mobile card-stack bulk selection — see §10) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #7's explicitly deferred items (field-mapping UI, background import dashboard, CSV export/import for other entities, Deal/Task creation from import rows, Kanban-side import navigation entry — see §11) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #8's explicitly deferred items (score/Deal/Task/duplicate-trend/import-trend reporting, monetary aggregates, charts, export, caching/precomputation, Client User visibility, time-bucketing beyond the daily-volume table — see §12) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #9's explicitly deferred items (event-driven triggers, additional condition types/fields, additional actions, multi-step/sequenced nurturing, per-lead workflow-state tracking, a rules-management UI, a visual workflow builder, a generic action dispatcher — see §13) remain intentionally deferred.
- A genuine, pre-existing PostgreSQL bug was found (not fixed) during Feature #9: `SendLeadAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` and `SendTaskAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` both query `notifications.data` (a plain `text` column) with a JSON-path operator that requires `json`/`jsonb` — confirmed to throw `SQLSTATE[42883]` against the real database every time. SQLite (the test driver) doesn't enforce this the same way, which is why the existing test suite never caught it. **Recommended as its own separate, explicitly-scoped future fix** — see §13 for full detail.
- Feature #10's explicitly deferred items (SLA target/deadline configuration, breach detection/persistence/alerts, any new scheduler/queue job, an SLA history table, assignment-response as a distinct metric, status-stage-duration reporting, daily first-response trend, response-time distribution/buckets, Client User visibility — see §14) remain intentionally deferred.
- Feature #11's explicitly deferred items (CSV import support for custom fields, bulk custom-field editing, custom-field filtering/search/sorting, reporting/dashboard breakdowns, webhook custom-field ingestion, Website Form integration, multi-select/checkbox field types, field-level permissions, retroactive backfill, a historical value-change timeline beyond the existing `LeadDetailUpdateLog` entry, a generic dynamic-form engine — see §15) remain intentionally deferred.

- Feature #13's explicitly deferred items (hosted/public forms, iframe embedding, a JS embed SDK, a drag-and-drop field builder, conditional field logic, multi-step forms, file uploads, CAPTCHA, advanced spam scoring, per-form analytics/conversion reporting, A/B testing, multi-language forms, campaign-specific forms, email automation, advanced workflow automation, webhook delivery retries, advanced styling/theming, per-domain allowlisting — see §17) remain intentionally deferred.
- A `tenant_code`-enumeration oracle exists on every public webhook resolver (`resolveSource()` and, inheriting the same pattern, Feature #13's `resolveWebsiteForm()`) — differing 404 exception messages let an anonymous requester distinguish "tenant_code doesn't exist" from "tenant_code exists." Pre-existing since before Feature #13; a uniform, non-model-revealing 404 body across every webhook resolver is a recommended future cross-cutting fix, not scoped to any single feature. Confirmed still present and unchanged as of Feature #14's closure.

If and when a new feature is explicitly requested, follow the same required process as every prior feature in this document: Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Proposal → **wait for explicit user approval** → Implementation → Testing → Regression Verification. Do not start coding immediately — perform the same depth of repository/schema/UI/permission inspection Features #1–#15 each did before proposing an architecture.

## 22. Important Instruction

A future Claude CLI session must treat this document as the authoritative Lead Management handoff state, **while still inspecting the actual current codebase before making implementation decisions**.

Never assume the codebase exactly matches this document — verify the current code first (files may have shifted, tests may have been added, a human may have made changes outside this workflow).

Do not reopen completed features merely because they are documented here.

## 23. Forensic Audit Remediation

Following Feature #15's closure, a full forensic audit of the entire Lead Management module (not limited to Feature #15) was conducted across two sessions: an initial audit (security, data integrity, concurrency/cross-feature, QA/dead-code — all read-only, no code changes) and a follow-up validation session that independently re-verified every material finding against the current codebase and reconciled the audit's finding inventory. Findings are tracked by ID (C1–C6, M1–M10, L1–L4, plus QA gaps Q1–Q9 and intentional/deferred items D1–D6) rather than as a numbered "Feature." Each approved fix follows the same Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Proposal → wait for explicit approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression Verification discipline as every Feature above, scoped to exactly **one finding at a time** — audit fixes are not to be bundled together.

### C5 — Raw Meta access token serialized into DownloadLeadAttachment's queued job payload — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `App\Jobs\DownloadLeadAttachment` took the Meta access token (and, for WhatsApp, the `phone_number_id`) as plain constructor scalars — neither intercepted by `SerializesModels`, so both were serialized in cleartext into the `jobs` table (queue driver: `database`) and, since the job's `$tries = 1`, permanently into `failed_jobs.payload` on any failure. This contradicted the "resolve credentials fresh inside the job, never pass them through `dispatch()`" convention already established elsewhere in this module (`WhatsAppSender`, `SendOutboundMessage`, `ReconcileOutboundMessageStatus`, `ProcessEmailLeadSource`).

**Fix**: Removed `accessToken`/`whatsappPhoneNumberId` from the job's constructor entirely. Added a private `resolveCredentials()` method that re-reads `$this->attachment->lead->source->config` at execution time — mirroring `WhatsAppSender::send()`'s existing pattern exactly. `LeadWebhookController::createPendingAttachmentAndDispatch()` no longer passes either value into `dispatch()`; its pre-dispatch token-existence guard is unchanged. If credentials can't be resolved at execution time (e.g. config cleared between dispatch and execution), the job now calls the existing `markFailed()` with a clear reason rather than crashing — the same visible-`Failed`-attachment outcome shape every other failure path in this job already produces.

**Scope deviation, explicitly approved during implementation**: `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookAttachmentTest.php` (not in the original approved file list) had 3 assertions directly referencing the removed `accessToken`/`whatsappPhoneNumberId` properties — a direct, unavoidable mechanical consequence of the constructor change, not an independent scope expansion. Implementation stopped and reported this before touching the file; the fix (replacing the value comparisons with `! property_exists($job, 'accessToken')`/`! property_exists($job, 'whatsappPhoneNumberId')` negative assertions) was explicitly approved before proceeding.

**Verification**:
- Focused `DownloadLeadAttachmentJobTest.php`: **12/12 passed** (6 existing tests mechanically updated to source the token via the lead's `LeadSource.config` instead of the constructor; 4 new tests — serialized-payload sentinel-token absence, credential-rotation-between-dispatch-and-execution, missing-credential-marks-Failed-safely, cross-tenant-credential-isolation).
- `WebhookAttachmentTest.php`: **6/6 passed**.
- Related attachment/Meta tests (`LeadAttachmentDownloadTest`, `LeadAttachmentDisplayTest`, `CleanupOrphanedLeadAttachmentsTest`, `WebhookFacebookLeadAdsTest`): **22/22 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **888/889 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1054/1057 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2; the skip is the same pre-existing skip documented throughout this file. Zero new failures.
- Pint: clean.
- PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; explicitly confirmed zero of those errors are in either changed production file (`app/Jobs/DownloadLeadAttachment.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php`).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: raw access token and `phone_number_id` are absent from the job's constructor and its serialized payload; credentials resolve fresh at execution time (proven by a rotation test); tenant isolation preserved (proven by a dedicated cross-tenant test); no schema/migration change; no queue-configuration change; `WhatsAppSender`/`MessengerSender`/`InstagramSender`/`ProcessEmailLeadSource`/other jobs untouched; `$tries = 1` and existing retry/failure semantics unchanged.

**C5 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression.

### C1 — Platform Admin LeadSource deletion has no dependent-data guard — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `App\Livewire\Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()` called `$source->delete()` unconditionally — no dependency check of any kind, unlike the tenant-side `Tenant\Leads\Index::deleteSource()`. Depending on which dependents existed, this either silently cascaded away every Lead (and its full history) for a source with no restrict-type dependent, or threw an uncaught `QueryException` for a source with one (`lead_source_costs`/`website_forms`/`conversations`, all `restrictOnDelete()`).

**Fix**: `delete()` now wraps the lookup, dependency checks, and the delete itself in one `DB::transaction()`, with `lockForUpdate()` applied to the `LeadSource` row before any check runs — closing the cascade-side check-then-act race (a webhook/import creating a new dependent between the check and the delete) while preserving PostgreSQL's own already-safe `restrictOnDelete()` guarantees. Five direct dependents are checked in this order, each with its own `Flux::toast('danger', ...)` and an early `return` before any deletion: **conversations → leads → lead_imports → lead_source_costs → website_forms**. Conversations are checked first since — per the related DE1 finding below — that dependency has no existing way to ever be cleared, unlike costs/forms (which a tenant can delete individually via existing UI) or leads/imports (which never block deletion at all, only cascade). Authorization (`Gate::forUser(...)->authorize('update', $this->tenant)`) and the tenant-scoped resolution (`$this->tenant->leadSources()`) are both unchanged in position and behavior — only `lockForUpdate()` was added to the resolution chain.

**DE1 — LeadSource records with inbound Conversation history can become permanently undeletable — explicitly investigated alongside C1, confirmed real, and deliberately NOT fixed here.** Verified during C1's own architecture inspection: `LeadSourceCost` and `WebsiteForm` rows can both be cleared via existing tenant UI, and `leads`/`lead_imports` never block deletion (cascade, not restrict) — but no delete, soft-delete, archive, or detach path exists anywhere in the codebase for `Conversation`/`Message`. Once a `LeadSource` has ever received one inbound WhatsApp/Messenger/Instagram message, it is now *correctly and visibly* refused deletion (via C1's fix) rather than crashing — but it remains, exactly as before, **permanently** undeletable. This is unchanged by C1 and is **intentionally deferred** pending a separate, explicit product decision (data-retention policy, whether to ever support archiving/re-parenting, etc. — none of that was implemented, backfilled, or decided in this work). `LeadSource.is_active`/`toggleActive()` (pre-existing, unmodified) remains the available lever for taking a source out of service without requiring deletion.

**Verification**:
- Focused `PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php` (new file): **11/11 passed** — empty-source success (with exact success-toast assertion), each of the five dependencies individually blocked (with exact danger-toast assertions), messages-survive-a-blocked-attempt, mixed-dependencies-blocked-by-the-first-checked-one, authorization-still-enforced, cross-tenant-isolation, and a `lockForUpdate()`-compiles-into-the-query test mirroring `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest.php`'s own established technique.
- `LeadPipelinePickerTest.php` (contains the one pre-existing Platform-deletion test, left untouched): **10/10 passed**.
- Tenant-side deletion + Currency-guard regression (`FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest`, `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest`, `LeadManagementSettingsTest`): **59/59 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **899/900 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
