No database trigger/constraint, no `SERIALIZABLE` isolation, no advisory lock, and no historical backfill were used — all evaluated and explicitly rejected in the Architecture Brief in favor of this codebase's own established `DB::transaction()` + row-lock idiom. `LeadPolicy` and every other writer's unrelated behavior were left untouched.

**`Index::applyBulkStatusChange()` — savepoint correction**: initially implemented as one outer transaction/lock for the whole bounded batch (≤`MAX_BULK_SELECTION` = 100 leads), which silently narrowed this method's own previously-documented guarantee ("one failure can't roll back everything else already applied") into "one failure rolls back the entire batch." Caught during Final Verification, not accepted as a silent trade-off: corrected so each lead's mutation still runs inside its own nested `DB::transaction()` — confirmed, via direct inspection of Laravel's transaction manager and empirically against this app's real Postgres connection (`DB::transactionLevel()` traced through a real success/failure/success sequence), to compile to a genuine `SAVEPOINT`/`ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` rather than a fresh `BEGIN` — wrapped in `try`/`catch (Throwable)` so a failure rolls back only that lead's own savepoint, is logged (mirroring `NurtureInactiveLeads`'s identical per-item isolation pattern), and lets the loop continue to the remaining leads. This preserves the original isolation guarantee for already-committed leads *and* improves on it (the original had no surrounding `try`/`catch`, so an uncaught exception aborted the whole loop; the corrected version continues), while the single outer lock/transaction — and therefore the coordination protocol above — is unchanged.

**No schema change, no migration, no product/business decision required**: the business rule itself ("don't remove a pipeline status while a lead is in it") was already established and approved in `toggleLeadStatus()`'s own pre-existing check; this closure only makes its enforcement atomic against the seven identified concurrent writers.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: 12 new tests in `tests/Feature/Leads/StatusPipelineLockCoordinationTest.php`. **3 are genuine, independently-confirmed fail-then-pass proofs** (reverting the corresponding production file alone reproduces the original bug): `toggleLeadStatus()`'s removal-under-lock re-validation ordering, the removal-only extra-lock-query count, and `CreateManualLead`'s fallback-instead-of-stranding behavior (which directly reproduces the finding's core bug — a foreign/removed status id passed straight through — against unfixed code). **1 is a genuine behavioral-regression proof** for the savepoint correction (reverting only the nested-transaction/`try`/`catch` reproduces the uncaught-exception, whole-batch-abort regression). **6 are structural/query-order guards** that pass even against a full revert of their own writer (Livewire's own per-request model rehydration, `Auth::user()->tenant`, and `Lead::create()`'s own pre-existing `nextIdentifier()` tenant-row lock each incidentally satisfy the same assertion shape in unfixed code) — explicitly not represented as concurrency proofs; the one load-bearing structural claim without its own discriminating test (`applyBulkStatusChange()`'s single-lock-for-the-whole-batch claim) is independently, empirically verified below instead. **2 are pre-existing-behavior tests**, unaffected by locking either way. PostgreSQL's actual `FOR SHARE`/`FOR UPDATE` blocking semantics — impossible to prove on this suite's SQLite driver, which silently strips any lock clause — were independently verified via two live PDO connections against this app's real database (`statement_timeout`-based, no sleep-based timing): both directions of mutual exclusion confirmed, 5/5. Two real, two-process concurrency tests were additionally run directly against the real Postgres connection using actual application code (`CreateManualLead`, the real `applyBulkStatusChange()` method, and a real `toggleLeadStatus()` removal): a writer racing a removal blocked for the removal's full duration and then correctly fell back instead of stranding a lead; a writer committing first correctly caused a subsequent removal to observe the new count and refuse; and — specifically for the savepoint correction — a concurrent removal attempt against an in-flight 3-lead bulk batch blocked for the batch's *entire* remaining duration (not just past the first lead's own savepoint), proving the lock spans every nested savepoint without release, then correctly refused once unblocked. All temporary test data created for these live-database verifications was deleted afterward.

**Verification**:
- `StatusPipelineLockCoordinationTest.php`: **12/12 passed**, 48 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **976/977 passed, 1 pre-existing (IMAP-extension) skip, 0 failures.**
- Real PostgreSQL lock semantics (`FOR SHARE`/`FOR UPDATE` mutual exclusion, both directions): **5/5 passed**, independently re-run at Final Verification.
- Real two-process races against the real Postgres connection, using real application writers: **passed in both directions**, including the `applyBulkStatusChange()` lock/savepoint-span case.
- Historical stranded-lead diagnostic (read-only, real database): **0 stranded leads found** — no remediation required, nothing deferred.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **423 errors** — 2 fewer than the pre-existing 425 baseline (incidental, from added explicit closure return types), zero new diagnostics, zero errors in any changed line. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Platform/Clients/LeadSources.php` (`toggleLeadStatus()` split into an unlocked addition branch and a locked removal branch), `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (`moveLead()`, `applyBulkStatusChange()` — lock + nested-savepoint correction), `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` (`updateStatus()`), `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateManualLead.php` (lock + re-check/fallback), `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php` (status resolution moved inside the existing per-row transaction, after the lock), `app/Jobs/ProcessEmailLeadSource.php` (new wrapping transaction + lock), `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (`createLead()` — new wrapping transaction + lock), `tests/Feature/Leads/StatusPipelineLockCoordinationTest.php` (12 new tests; no existing test modified).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `LeadPolicy`, `LeadAssignmentService`'s own `TenantUser` round-robin/least-loaded locking, every migration/cascade FK definition, every previously-closed TOCTOU finding's implementation (C1, tenant `deleteSource()`, Platform Settings `deleteStatus()`/`deleteCurrency()`, `LeadCustomFields::delete()`), and `toggleCurrency()` (a lower-severity, explicitly out-of-scope sibling — no FK-independent invariant is bypassed there) were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, and `toggleCurrency()`'s own lower-severity locking gap remain explicitly deferred/open — none are resolved by this closure.

### `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::toggleCurrency()` — TOCTOU Race Allowing a Tenant to Reach Zero Enabled Currencies — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — the previously-deferred sibling observation from the `toggleLeadStatus()` closure above (explicitly named there as a lower-severity, out-of-scope gap), freshly re-confirmed against the current codebase and closed via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding / root cause**: `toggleCurrency()`'s removal branch checked `count($currentIds) <= 1` against a plain, unlocked read, then called `$this->tenant->currencies()->detach($currencyId)` as a separate, later, unguarded statement. Two concurrent removals of two *different* currently-enabled currencies — when the tenant had exactly two enabled — could each read `count() == 2` and pass the guard before either committed, leaving the tenant with **zero** enabled currencies, violating the method's own documented invariant ("the one invariant the tenant must always satisfy: at least one currency enabled"). Unlike the `toggleLeadStatus()` finding, this does not strand or corrupt any `Lead` data — the impact is an availability gap: `LeadSources::create()`/`save()` both already refuse to let a zero-currency tenant create a new Lead Source, so the race silently blocks that capability until a Platform Admin notices and re-enables a currency. Genuinely reproduced this session via a `DB::listen()` interception against the real Postgres connection, confirming a final count of exactly 0.

**Fix — single-writer Tenant-row exclusive lock**: a fresh, exhaustive search confirmed `toggleCurrency()` is the *sole* application writer capable of removing a tenant's currency — `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()` is additive-only (guarded by `if ($tenant->currencies()->exists()) return;`), and no other writer, raw `DB::table('tenant_currencies')` write, or removal path exists anywhere in the codebase. This meant no multi-writer shared/exclusive coordination protocol (unlike `toggleLeadStatus()`'s seven writers) was needed — the removal branch alone was wrapped in `DB::transaction()`, acquiring an exclusive `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` on the tenant row (the same row `toggleLeadStatus()` and `Lead::nextIdentifier()` already lock) as its first statement, then re-reading the enabled-currency IDs from that locked instance before re-evaluating both whether the target is still selected (safe no-op if a concurrent call already removed it) and whether it's the last one remaining (existing, byte-for-byte unchanged "at least one enabled currency" toast). `detach()` only runs once both checks pass. The addition branch remains entirely outside the lock, mirroring `toggleLeadStatus()`'s own addition branch — adding a currency can never violate the invariant. `selectedCurrencies` cache invalidation was restructured to fire only on an actual `detach()` (via a `bool` returned from the transaction closure), precisely preserving the original method's own control flow, where the refusal path never reached that call either — a subtlety caught and corrected during implementation, not a silent behavior change.

**No schema change, no migration, no product/business decision required**: the invariant was already established in the method's own docblock and already enforced (non-atomically) downstream; this closure only makes its enforcement atomic.

**Regression test, verified in both directions**: one new test in `tests/Feature/Leads/TenantCurrencyPickerTest.php` (8 pre-existing tests unmodified), using the same `DB::listen()`-interception technique as this module's prior closures, isolated specifically to `toggleCurrency()`'s own currency-ID read (mounting the component separately first, avoiding a false-negative trap where Livewire's own mount-time reads would otherwise be intercepted instead — caught and corrected during Impact Analysis). **Genuine fail-then-pass proof performed twice, independently** (once during implementation, once again during Final Verification): reverting only the production fix reproduces a real, non-tautological failure (no refusal toast fires, and independently confirmed the underlying currency count reaches 0); the restored fix passes, leaving exactly one currency enabled. Two real two-process concurrency tests were additionally run directly against the real Postgres connection, using the actual `toggleCurrency()` method (not a reimplementation), covering both race orderings: a removal holding the lock while a second concurrent removal blocks and then correctly refuses once unblocked; and a removal that commits first, correctly causing a subsequent removal to observe the reduced count and refuse. In both orderings the final state was confirmed, directly against the database, to be exactly one currency remaining — never zero. All temporary verification data was deleted afterward.

**Verification**:
- `TenantCurrencyPickerTest.php`: **9/9 passed**, 19 assertions.
- Broader `LeadSources.php`-related tests (10 files): **74/74 passed**, 202 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **977/978 passed, 1 pre-existing (IMAP-extension) skip, 0 failures.**
- Real PostgreSQL two-process races, using the actual production method, both orderings: confirmed — final state never zero.
- Historical zero-currency diagnostic (read-only, real database): **0 tenants with zero enabled currencies found** — no remediation required.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **423 errors** — matching the current baseline exactly, zero new diagnostics, zero errors in any changed line. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Platform/Clients/LeadSources.php` (`toggleCurrency()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/TenantCurrencyPickerTest.php` (1 new test; no existing test modified).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `toggleLeadStatus()` and all 7 of its status-writing paths, `Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteCurrency()` (global catalog delete), `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`, `LeadSources::create()`, `LeadSources::save()`, `LeadValidationRules`, and every migration/trigger were not touched. Two adjacent, narrower, pre-existing observations were explicitly left unaddressed, named rather than silently ignored: `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`'s own unrelated duplicate-attach race (a concurrent double module-assignment could hit the `unique(tenant_id, currency_id)` constraint — cannot cause a zero-currency state, not part of this invariant) and `LeadSources::save()`'s own narrower pre-existing check-then-persist gap at Lead Source persistence time.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`'s duplicate-attach race, and `LeadSources::save()`'s narrower check-then-persist gap remain explicitly deferred/open — none are resolved by this closure.

### `App\Actions\Platform\AssignModuleToTenant::handle()` — Concurrent Duplicate Module Assignment — **DEFERRED / ACCEPTABLE CONCURRENT CONFLICT — NO IMPLEMENTATION REQUIRED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — selected via the standard finding-selection cycle (same TOCTOU/concurrency sweep that produced the `toggleLeadStatus()`/`toggleCurrency()` closures above) and evaluated via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis → User Decision sequence. This finding does **not** proceed through Architecture Brief / Implementation / Testing / Final Verification, because the Impact Analysis concluded, and the user explicitly approved, that no code change is warranted.

**Original finding**: `handle()`'s single `DB::transaction()` performs five unguarded check-then-write sequences against the same tenant — `tenant_modules` (via `syncWithoutDetaching()`), `role_modules` (via `syncWithoutDetaching()`), `role_module_permissions` (delete + bulk insert), and, when the module is `lead-management`, `tenant_lead_statuses` and `tenant_currencies` (both via `attach()`, each guarded only by an unlocked `exists()` check in `seedDefaultPipeline()`/`seedDefaultCurrency()`) — with no row lock. This is the same check-then-act shape as every TOCTOU race fixed in this module, and it is what `toggleCurrency()`'s own closure above named as an adjacent, unaddressed observation (`AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`'s duplicate-attach race).

**Evidence gathered**: genuinely reproduced using two separate OS processes/connections against the real PostgreSQL database (not a same-connection `DB::listen()` interception — an initial same-connection attempt was identified as producing a misleading nested-`SAVEPOINT` result and was discarded in favor of true cross-connection concurrency, using a deterministic `pg_sleep()`-based pause, the same controlled technique used for the real-Postgres proofs in the two closures above). Result: the losing transaction threw `Illuminate\Database\UniqueConstraintViolationException` on `tenant_modules_tenant_id_module_id_unique` and rolled back completely, with the database showing **zero** partial/orphaned rows in any of the five affected tables; the winning transaction committed all five writes correctly and completely (`tenant_modules`, `tenant_lead_statuses` (8 rows), `tenant_currencies` (1 row) all confirmed as the winner's own complete work). Every one of the five affected tables carries a real unique constraint (`tenant_modules`, `role_modules`, `role_module_permissions`, `tenant_lead_statuses`, `tenant_currencies` — all confirmed from the active migrations), so a collision can never silently corrupt data: it always either serializes cleanly or throws.

**Decision — DEFERRED / ACCEPTABLE CONCURRENT CONFLICT, no implementation required**: the sole caller, `Platform\Clients\Show::toggleModule()`, already catches `QueryException` and shows a controlled danger toast (proven by the existing, unmodified, passing `ClientModuleAssignmentFailureTest.php` — no new test was needed or added). This codebase's own established convention for structurally identical low-contention "avoid duplicate provisioning" scenarios (`firstOrCreate()`/`updateOrCreate()` in `LeadDuplicateLink`, `Conversation`, `LeadSource`, `LeadConsent`, `LeadCustomFieldValue`) does not use explicit locking either. Adding a `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` (the idiom used in every prior closure in this section) would only convert "one clean, caught failure plus one success" into "two successes" — a discretionary idempotency/UX improvement, not a correction of unsafe or incorrect behavior. No security, authorization, or data-integrity defect exists here: unlike `toggleLeadStatus()`'s finding, there is no FK-independent invariant that can be silently violated; unlike both prior closures, the failure is always loud and always fully, atomically rolled back.

**Conditions that would justify revisiting this decision**: (1) evidence of meaningful real-world collision frequency (e.g., repeated `tenant_modules_tenant_id_module_id_unique` violations observed in logs/support reports); (2) a future product or API requirement that concurrent/duplicate module-assignment requests must both succeed idempotently (e.g., a programmatic/automation caller that cannot tolerate a retry-required failure); (3) a future change that materially increases contention on this action, such as bulk/batch module assignment fired concurrently across many tenants.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `toggleLeadStatus()`, `toggleCurrency()`, `RemoveModuleFromTenant` (naturally idempotent — a delete is a no-op on an already-absent row, no unique-constraint risk), `Show::toggleModule()`'s own click-handling, and every other finding in this section were not touched; no production code, test, or migration was modified. This finding is explicitly distinguished from `toggleLeadStatus()` and `toggleCurrency()` above: both of those involved a **silent** invariant violation (a lead stranded outside its tenant's pipeline with no FK to catch it, or a tenant reaching zero enabled currencies) that required architectural change; this finding involves a **caught, loud, fully-rolled-back** transactional conflict that the application already handles correctly, so it does not.

**This finding is DEFERRED, not implemented.** It is not carried forward as an open item in the current security/data-integrity remediation cycle. Do not reopen without new evidence matching one of the three conditions above. `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`'s duplicate-attach race, named as a deferred observation in the `toggleCurrency()` closure above, is superseded by and fully accounted for within this evaluation (it is one of the five write sequences assessed here) — it remains an accepted, non-implemented, non-open item under this same disposition, not a separately-tracked gap.

### `App\Actions\Tenant\RecordLeadConsent::handle()` — Hypothesized Concurrent Consent Race — **FALSE POSITIVE — NO IMPLEMENTATION REQUIRED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — selected via the standard finding-selection cycle on the hypothesis that `handle()`'s `LeadConsent::updateOrCreate()` call (inside `DB::transaction()`, alongside a `LeadConsentEvent::create()`) could throw an uncaught `UniqueConstraintViolationException` when two concurrent grant/withdraw calls raced for the same `lead_id`+`consent_type` (a real `unique(['lead_id', 'consent_type'])` constraint exists on `lead_consents`), with neither of its two callers (`Show::mutateConsent()`, `LeadWebhookController::websiteFormSubmit()`) catching it. Evaluated via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis sequence; does not proceed to Architecture Brief / Implementation / Testing / Final Verification, because the Impact Analysis disproved the premise.

**Disproven by vendor-source inspection and a genuine two-process PostgreSQL reproduction**: direct inspection of the installed Laravel version's `Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder` confirmed `updateOrCreate()` calls `firstOrCreate()` → `createOrFirst()`, which wraps its `create()` attempt in `withSavepointIfNeeded()` and explicitly catches `UniqueConstraintViolationException`, re-querying for the row a concurrent writer just committed instead of propagating the exception; `withSavepointIfNeeded()` detects it is already inside `RecordLeadConsent::handle()`'s own `DB::transaction()` and wraps the attempt in a nested transaction, which compiles to a real Postgres `SAVEPOINT` (the same mechanism independently confirmed during the `toggleLeadStatus()` closure), so only the failed `create()` attempt unwinds — not the surrounding transaction or its subsequent `LeadConsentEvent::create()` call. This was then verified dynamically, not just read: two genuinely separate OS processes, marker-file-synchronized (not sleep-timing-guessed) so T2 provably ran to completion while T1 was held inside a deterministic `pg_sleep()` pause, both called the real `RecordLeadConsent::handle()` for the same lead+type. **Neither transaction threw.** Both reported success; the losing side's write became a clean `UPDATE` of the winning side's already-committed row.

**Final database state remained fully correct**: exactly one `lead_consents` row survived (the invariant held, no duplicate), and **both** `lead_consent_events` audit rows survived (nothing lost) — confirmed by direct query against the real database. No partial, orphaned, or compliance-significant state loss occurred. Consequently, the downstream scenarios the finding was selected to investigate do not occur either: `websiteFormSubmit()` never receives an exception from this race (so no HTTP 500, and no client-retry-driven duplicate `Lead` risk), and `Show::mutateConsent()`'s Livewire path likewise never receives an uncaught exception from it.

**No production fix was required; no files were modified.** This finding is explicitly distinguished from the genuine races fixed earlier in this section (`toggleLeadStatus()`, `toggleCurrency()`) and from the genuinely-real-but-deferred `AssignModuleToTenant::handle()` race above: those involve actual check-then-act sequences (pivot-table `attach()`/`sync()`/`syncWithoutDetaching()` via `InteractsWithPivotTable`, which is **not** protected the way `updateOrCreate()` is) where a real race either silently corrupts state or throws uncaught; here, the specific write this finding was selected over — a top-level Eloquent `updateOrCreate()` call — is natively race-safe in the installed Laravel version, so the hypothesized defect does not exist. A secondary, narrower observation surfaced during analysis — that the surviving current-state row reflects database commit order rather than client-submission order when a Grant and a Withdraw genuinely race each other — is **not** presented as a confirmed defect or open action item: it is standard, generic concurrent-request behavior consistent with `LeadConsent`'s own documented design ("safely replaced ... by a later grant or withdrawal"), with no repository evidence (test, docblock, or requirement) asserting submission-order consistency is required.

**This finding is CLOSED as a FALSE POSITIVE.** Do not reopen without new evidence that the installed Laravel version's `createOrFirst()` behavior has changed (e.g., a framework upgrade) or that a different, not-yet-identified write path bypasses this protection.

### `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::resendMessage()` — Duplicate Resend / Duplicate Outbound Delivery — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — selected via the standard finding-selection cycle. Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding**: `resendMessage()` had no idempotency protection of any kind, unlike its sibling `sendMessage()` (which uses `Cache::lock()`). Any number of resend attempts against the **same** failed `Message` — concurrent *or* simply sequential repeat clicks, since the "Resend" button's visibility depended only on the original row's own `status === 'failed'`, which `resendMessage()` never mutated — each independently created a brand-new `Message` row and independently reached a real external send (`WhatsAppSender`/`MessengerSender`/`InstagramSender`, none of which pass any idempotency key to the provider). Dynamically confirmed, both during Impact Analysis and again during Implementation's own pre-fix reproduction, via genuine two-OS-process/two-Postgres-connection races against the real database: two (and in one incidental case, three) concurrent resend attempts against one failed message each independently succeeded, producing multiple `sent` rows with distinct external message ids for identical body text — a real, externally-visible duplicate customer message on a paid business-messaging channel, not merely an internal data anomaly.

**Fix — persisted `resend_message_id` + `DB::transaction()` + `lockForUpdate()`**: a new nullable, self-referencing `messages.resend_message_id` column (added via `database/migrations/2026_09_21_060000_add_resend_message_id_to_messages_table.php`, `nullOnDelete()`, the same forward-pointing self-reference shape as `Lead.merged_into_lead_id`) records, on the *original* Failed row, which resend was created from it — `NULL` means "not yet resent." `resendMessage()` now locks that row (`Message::lockForUpdate()`) inside one `DB::transaction()`, checks `resend_message_id !== null` under the lock (gracefully rejecting with a new warning toast — "This message has already been resent." — if already set), re-runs the existing eligibility check, creates the resend row exactly as before, then links the original via a **raw** `DB::table('messages')->where(...)->update([...])` in the same transaction — deliberately not `$failedMessage->update()`, and `resend_message_id` is deliberately excluded from `#[Fillable]`, mirroring `LeadMergeService`'s own established `merged_into_lead_id` convention (must only ever be set by this one code path, never accepted through a generic payload). `SendOutboundMessage::dispatch()` is called only after `DB::transaction()` returns a non-null `Message` — i.e. only once already committed — never from inside the closure, mirroring `sendMessage()`'s own existing create-then-dispatch ordering; no change to the global `after_commit` queue config was made or needed. The invariant enforced is **exactly one resend per Failed row**, applied uniformly: if a resend itself later fails, it becomes its own independently-Failed row and is, under the identical rule, itself resendable exactly once — "retry after a resend also fails" falls out of the existing per-row Resend-button rendering with no special-cased chain logic. `Cache::lock()` was deliberately **not** added alongside the database lock — evaluated and rejected as redundant, consistent with every prior TOCTOU closure in this module, which rely on `lockForUpdate()` alone.

**No product/business decision required beyond the approved architecture**: additive-only migration, no backfill, every pre-existing `Message` row starts `NULL` (correctly meaning "not yet resent").

**Verification**:
- `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadMessageComposerTest.php`: **11/11 passed**, 43 assertions (1 existing test extended, 4 new tests added: second-sequential-resend rejected with no second row; a real `Message::created` listener forcing a mid-transaction throw proves the resend row and the original's link roll back together, no orphan, no false marker; a resend that itself fails becomes independently resendable exactly once; the Resend button hides once `resend_message_id` is set). Re-run independently at Final Verification with identical results.
- Genuine two-process PostgreSQL race, re-verified twice (once during Implementation, once independently at Final Verification, fresh fixture each time): two separate OS processes/connections, marker-file-synchronized (not sleep-timing-guessed), both contending `Message::lockForUpdate()` on the same real Failed row — final database state both times: **exactly one original failed row + one resend row + one `resend_message_id` link + zero duplicate resend rows.** The losing process's transaction correctly observed the just-committed marker and created nothing.
- Sequential (non-concurrent) duplicate resend on the same Livewire component instance: also correctly rejected — the defect was not concurrency-only.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression): **1006/1009 passed** (3 runs), the 2 failures being the pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions (independently reconfirmed via `git stash` to fail identically against unmodified `main`). One additional run showed an intermittent `KanbanBoardTest` failure (`a client user's board search does not match location...`), investigated and root-caused to that test's own pre-existing fragility — its factory-random `phone` value occasionally contains the literal search substring being asserted absent — reproduces in isolation-unrelated conditions, did not recur in 3 of 4 runs, and has no code-path connection to any file this fix touched; left untouched per scope.
- `sendMessage()` and `app/Jobs/SendOutboundMessage.php`: confirmed via `git diff` to have **zero changed lines** — neither was modified.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan on the two changed PHP files: **60 errors, identical count and identical set before and after this change** (re-measured via `git stash` on the same two files) — zero new diagnostics introduced, pre-existing baseline untouched. The new migration analyses clean (0 errors).
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `database/migrations/2026_09_21_060000_add_resend_message_id_to_messages_table.php` (new), `app/Models/Message.php` (`resend()` relation, PHPDoc; `resend_message_id` deliberately excluded from `#[Fillable]`), `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` (`resendMessage()` only), `resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads/partials/message-composer-card.blade.php` (Resend button condition only), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadMessageComposerTest.php`.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `sendMessage()`, `SendOutboundMessage`, `OutboundEligibilityService`, `MessageSenderFactory`, and every `*Sender` implementation were not touched — no provider-level idempotency was introduced, per approved scope. Existing authorization (`Gate::denies`) and rate-limiting (`passesOutboundRateLimits()`) behavior on `resendMessage()` are unchanged code paths and remain covered by the same passing tests.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, `LeadSources::save()`'s narrower check-then-persist gap, and `AssignModuleToTenant::handle()`'s deferred concurrent-conflict finding remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.
