### `App\Http\Controllers\Api\LeadWebhookController` — Inbound Webhook Message Redelivery / Duplicate Append — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — selected via the standard finding-selection cycle. Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding**: `appendFacebookMessage()`, `appendInstagramMessage()`, and `appendWhatsappMessage()` (the existing-lead follow-up path inside `facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()`), plus `createPendingAttachmentAndDispatch()`, mutated `Lead.message`/`Lead.source_data['messages']` and created `LeadAttachment`/dispatched `DownloadLeadAttachment` unconditionally, with no check against the platform's own inbound message id. Meta/WhatsApp Cloud API webhooks are documented at-least-once delivery, so a genuine redelivery of the same inbound message — sequential or concurrent — duplicated this legacy content and dispatched a second real attachment download. The only existing protection, `messages.external_message_id` (unique per `tenant_id`+`channel`), sat at the *end* of the sequence, inside `RecordInboundMessage::handle()`, called from `recordInboundMessage()`'s own `try { ... } catch (Throwable $e) { Log::warning(...) }` — by the time its unique-constraint violation was caught and silently logged, `Lead.message`/`source_data` had already been mutated and saved, and any attachment row/job had already been created/dispatched. Dynamically confirmed, both sequentially (two real HTTP-kernel-dispatched webhook deliveries against the real Postgres dev database) and concurrently (a genuine two-OS-process, two-Postgres-connection, marker-file-synchronized race): the existing per-sender `withDedupeLock()` correctly prevented a duplicate `Lead`, but the legacy append still duplicated even when the lock worked exactly as designed, because the lock is a pure mutex with no idempotency check inside it — confirmed via direct inspection of `Illuminate\Cache\Lock::block()`'s source that it releases the instant its callback completes, not after the lock's TTL, so a sequential redelivery arriving even slightly later than the first request's own processing time was already unprotected regardless of the nominal 10-second `lockSeconds`.

**Fix — durable idempotency gate reusing the existing unique index**: a new private `alreadyRecordedMessage(LeadSource $source, MessageChannel $channel, ?string $externalMessageId): bool` queries `Message::where('tenant_id', $source->tenant_id)->where('channel', $channel)->where('external_message_id', $externalMessageId)->exists()` — the same columns the real `messages_external_id_unique` constraint already covers, no new table or column. Called as the literal first statement inside each channel's existing per-sender `withDedupeLock()` closure (`facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, and the shared `$handleMessage` closure in `whatsapp()`), before `existingLeadForConversation()`, before the legacy fallback lookup, before `append*Message()`, and before `createLead()` — so both the existing-lead and new-lead branches are protected by one check, not two. A `null` `$externalMessageId` (a payload that never carried a platform message id — "purely opportunistic," per `RecordInboundMessage`'s own docblock) short-circuits to `false` immediately, preserving today's existing, already-accepted behavior for that case unchanged; no identifier is synthesized from body/sender/timestamp. Mirrors `processFacebookLeadAd()`'s own `alreadyProcessed` check exactly — the same "durable existence check, first thing inside the existing sender-scoped lock" idiom already established and tested elsewhere in this same controller, not a new pattern.

**No migration, no new dedupe table, no new lock primitive**: the existing `withDedupeLock()` (unchanged `lockSeconds`/`blockSeconds`) is reused as-is for concurrency; `RecordInboundMessage` and its own decoupled error-tolerant transaction are entirely unmodified; `Cache::lock()` is not used anywhere new.

**Verification**:
- `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php`: **35/35 passed**, 168 assertions (6 new tests: Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp duplicate-mid/id redelivery, an attachment duplicate-redelivery case asserting exactly one `LeadAttachment` and one `DownloadLeadAttachment` dispatch, a duplicate-first-message case asserting exactly one `Lead` and one `LeadReceived`, and a positive-control case proving two genuinely different message ids still both append). The 3 pre-existing "follow-up message... appended instead of duplicated" tests (Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp) and the 3 "...lead has since been merged..." tests pass unmodified — confirmed their fixtures carry no `mid`/`id` field at all, so the new gate is a provable no-op against them.
- Genuine two-process PostgreSQL race, re-verified twice (Implementation and Final Verification, fresh fixture each time), same sender/message id/channel: final database state both times — **exactly one Lead, one normalized `Message` row, one legacy append** (`Lead.message`/`source_data['messages']` not duplicated); the losing process's re-check (after acquiring the lock post-release) correctly found the just-committed row and exited without mutation.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression): **1012/1015 passed**, the 2 failures being the pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions (same baseline already established in the `resendMessage()` closure above), plus 1 pre-existing IMAP-extension skip — neither touched, neither part of this finding.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan on `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php`: **0 errors before and 0 errors after** (re-measured via `git stash`) — no new diagnostic.
- `git diff --check`: clean. No real external Meta/WhatsApp API was called at any point — `Queue::fake()`/`Http`-free fixtures throughout.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (`facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, `whatsapp()`, and the new `alreadyRecordedMessage()` helper only), `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php`.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `RecordInboundMessage`, `existingLeadForConversation()`'s merge-continuity behavior, `VerifyMetaWebhookSignature`, `processFacebookLeadAd()`'s own `alreadyProcessed` check, and every previously-closed finding in this section were not touched. Null-external-id behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged from before this closure.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, `LeadSources::save()`'s narrower check-then-persist gap, and `AssignModuleToTenant::handle()`'s deferred concurrent-conflict finding remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure. The pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and IMAP-extension skip noted above are unrelated, pre-existing, and were not addressed here.

### L2 — Unbounded Logical Webhook Message/Entry Processing Per Request — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED (application-level scope only)**

Original audit finding — `L2`, one of the four items (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) explicitly carried forward as not-yet-approved-for-implementation as of the audit-status reconciliation performed earlier this session. Original wording: *"No application-level payload-size limits on any webhook route... Left to PHP/webserver config. LOW CONFIDENCE, standard practice."* Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Scope clarification, established during Impact Analysis and preserved through closure**: the original finding's literal raw-HTTP-byte-size question (Nginx `client_max_body_size`, PHP `post_max_size`, or equivalent) is genuinely infrastructure/deployment configuration with **zero representation in this repository** (no `.htaccess` body-size directive, no `nginx.conf`, no `Dockerfile`, no `php.ini` found in-repo) — that portion remains an **open, separate, operational/infrastructure-verification item, explicitly not represented as fixed by this closure**. What this closure fixes is a distinct, more precisely evidenced application-level defect discovered during the same Impact Analysis: **unbounded logical message/entry count per webhook request**, independent of raw body size.

**Original finding (application-level, as measured)**: `facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, and `whatsapp()` had no ceiling on how many logical inbound messages a single request's JSON payload could claim to contain — every message triggered full synchronous processing (dedupe-lock cycle, DB lookups, `Lead`/`Conversation`/`Message` creation, auto-assignment, notifications). **Dynamically measured** (real local HTTP kernel dispatch, real Postgres dev database, bounded and reversible, no real external API calls): a 36KB WhatsApp-shaped payload containing 200 distinct-sender messages produced **~8 seconds of synchronous processing and 200 real `Lead` + 200 real `Message` rows**, from a single request, on a route with no rate limiter and no count cap of any kind. This demonstrated that a raw body-size limit — even if one exists at the infrastructure layer — would not have prevented this specific, measured risk, since 36KB is far below any plausible byte-size ceiling.

**Fix — configurable, total-per-request logical message-count cap**: a new `config/webhooks.php` → `max_inbound_messages_per_request` (env `WEBHOOK_MAX_INBOUND_MESSAGES_PER_REQUEST`, default `100` — no repository or provider evidence supports a specific number; `100` mirrors this app's own existing `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index::MAX_BULK_SELECTION` precedent for "a reasonable single-request work ceiling," not a documented platform limit). Checked as the literal first statement inside `facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()`, before `resolveSource()`, before any per-message lock/DB/API work. Counting semantics: Facebook/Instagram sum `entry[].messaging[]` items across **every** entry in the request (not per-entry, closing the "split across many entries" evasion), excluding delivery/read receipts; WhatsApp sums `entry[].changes[].value.messages[]` items, excluding `value.statuses[]` (the separate, cheaper outbound-status-reconciliation path) — WhatsApp reuses the already-computed message list from the pre-existing `summarizeWhatsappIncoming()` diagnostic-log helper rather than walking the payload a second time. An over-limit request is rejected **in full — no partial processing** — logged as a warning, and still acknowledged with the controller's pre-existing `HTTP 200 {"status":"ok"}` response, matching this controller's own established "every failure mode returns 200" convention (documented and tested elsewhere in this same file specifically to avoid Meta's own webhook-retry-on-non-2xx behavior). The recently-closed inbound redelivery idempotency gate (`alreadyRecordedMessage()`, `tenant_id`+`channel`+`external_message_id`) is entirely untouched — the new cap check runs strictly earlier, not in place of it.

**No migration, no new queue, no new lock primitive, no signature-verification change**: `VerifyMetaWebhookSignature` and its fail-open-per-source design are unmodified; `RecordInboundMessage` is unmodified; the existing per-sender `withDedupeLock()` is reused as-is.

**Verification**:
- `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php`: **49/49 passed**, 210 assertions (14 new tests: default-value assertion; under-cap/at-cap/over-cap for all three channels; total-across-entries counting; delivery/read-receipt and WhatsApp-statuses exclusion; a duplicate-id-inside-an-under-cap-batch interaction test proving the existing idempotency gate and the new cap compose correctly; attachment-bearing and null-external-id regression checks).
- **Genuine real-Postgres boundary verification, independently re-run twice** (Implementation and Final Verification): against the actual shipped default of 100 (no config override), a request with exactly 100 messages was **fully accepted (100 Leads created)**; a request with exactly 101 messages was **fully rejected (0 Leads created)**, both returning HTTP 200 — no off-by-one. All fixture data deleted afterward; the dev database confirmed back to its exact pre-existing baseline both times.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression): **1026/1029 passed** (two independent runs, Implementation and Final Verification), the 2 failures being the pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions (same baseline established in prior closures in this section), plus 1 pre-existing IMAP-extension skip — neither touched.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan on `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` and `config/webhooks.php`: **0 errors before and 0 errors after** (re-measured via `git stash`, independently confirmed twice) — no new diagnostic.
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (`facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, `whatsapp()`, and two new private helpers `exceedsInboundMessageCap()`/`countMessagingItems()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php`, `config/webhooks.php` (new).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `VerifyMetaWebhookSignature`, `RecordInboundMessage`, `existingLeadForConversation()`'s merge-continuity behavior, `alreadyRecordedMessage()` and its `withDedupeLock()` placement, and every previously-closed finding in this section were not touched. No queue migration, no new lock, no infrastructure/server configuration was implemented in this repository.

**This finding is CLOSED at the application level only.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The original finding's raw HTTP-body-size question **remains open as a separate infrastructure/operational item**, outside this repository's scope — it is not resolved by this closure and should not be represented as such. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, `LeadSources::save()`'s narrower check-then-persist gap, and `AssignModuleToTenant::handle()`'s deferred concurrent-conflict finding remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded. M6, M9, L3, and L4 (the sibling not-yet-approved items from the same audit-status reconciliation) remain open and unaddressed by this closure. The pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and IMAP-extension skip noted above are unrelated, pre-existing, and were not addressed here.

### M6 — CSV Import Per-Row N+1 / Redundant Database Reads — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Original forensic audit finding `M6` — one of the four items (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) carried forward as not-yet-approved-for-implementation as of the audit-status reconciliation performed earlier this session (L2 has since been closed above; this closes M6). Selected for its own full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Edge-Case Decision → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Test-Quality Correction → Approval sequence. **Finding status: CONFIRMED** — not a false positive, not already mitigated: directly and dynamically measured, not speculative.

**Nature of the defect — performance/scalability and queue-worker resource contention, explicitly NOT a correctness or security issue**: `LeadCsvImport::model()` (called once per CSV row by Laravel Excel's `ToModel` contract) re-fetched `LeadImport`, `Tenant`, and `LeadSource` from scratch on every row, and `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` re-loaded a freshly-created `Lead`'s `source`/`status` relations a second time — all despite this data being invariant for the life of one import. **Measured baseline** (real Postgres dev database, real production entry point, `DB::listen()`-tallied): 10 rows → 195 queries (19.5/row); 200 rows → 3,805 queries (19.03/row) — confirmed linear, not quadratic, scaling. Bounded by the pre-existing `MAX_DATA_ROWS = 10000` ceiling and run off the request cycle via a queue worker (`ShouldQueue`, chunked), so the real-world impact is queue-worker occupancy and delay to every other job type sharing this application's single, unnamed `default` queue — not an HTTP-availability or data-integrity risk.

**Fix — approved minimal scope, chunk-scoped caching only**:
- `LeadCsvImport` lazily resolves and caches `LeadImport`, `Tenant`, and `LeadSource` once per `ReadChunk` job execution (`chunkSize()` = 200 rows) — **explicitly chunk-scoped, not a whole-import shared cache**: verified directly against the installed `maatwebsite/excel` vendor source (`Jobs/ReadChunk.php`) that each chunk is dispatched as its own separately queued, separately serialized job, so a fresh `LeadCsvImport` instance (these three properties back at `null`) is genuinely what every chunk starts from in production. No `BeforeImport` hook or any other cross-serialization-boundary state was used, precisely to avoid relying on unverified queue-serialization internals for cached Eloquent models.
- `TenantUser` (`imported_by`) is **deliberately NOT cached** — confirmed via exhaustive delete-path analysis that `LeadImport`/`Tenant` have no delete action anywhere in the application and `LeadSource` can never be deleted while any `LeadImport` references it (`Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()`'s own C1-closed guard), making those three provably safe to cache for an import's entire lifetime — while `TenantUser` has no such guard and `lead_imports.imported_by`/`lead_assignments.assigned_by`/`lead_lead_tag.tagged_by` are all `nullOnDelete()`, a real, application-permitted scenario. `TenantUser::find()` remains an unconditional, unmodified per-row lookup.
- `CreateLeadFromImport::processRow()` sets the already-resolved `source`/`status` instances directly onto the freshly-`Lead::create()`'d lead via `setRelation()`, and calls `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` with a new, **opt-in, default-`false`** `$trustLoadedRelations` parameter — every other of the 8 existing `computeFor()` callers (`Show.php` ×3, `Index.php` ×2, `LeadMergeService`, `CreateManualLead`, `RecalculateLeadScores`, `RecomputeLeadScoreOnReceipt`) is unmodified and continues using the default `load()` (forced reload), preserving the exact stale-relation protection that default exists for.

**Verified query reduction** (re-measured independently three times this session — Implementation, Final Verification, and again after the test-quality correction — all consistent):
- N=10: 195 → **148** queries (**24.1% reduction**)
- N=200: 3,805 → **2,808** queries (**26.2% reduction**)
- N=250 (new, crosses the 200-row chunk boundary): **3,511** queries (14.04/row) — `imported_count = 250`, `duplicate_count = 0`, `failed_count = 0`, every row number 1–250 present exactly once; `tenants`/`lead_sources` each queried **exactly 2 times** (`⌈250/200⌉`), confirming the per-chunk cache correctly resets at the boundary rather than persisting across it or never resolving; `tenant_users` queried **exactly 250 times**, confirming `TenantUser` freshness is fully preserved even across chunk boundaries.

**Query-count regression test and threshold**: `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php`'s new `'a moderate CSV import stays within the expected post-optimization query budget'` test, run through the real Livewire upload → `Excel::import()` path with `DB::listen()`. Measured actual: **299** queries for a 20-row fixture. Initial threshold (`< 340`) was reviewed at Final Verification and found to have a real, calculated blind spot — a `computeFor()`-only reversion would land at ≈339 (`< 340` would incorrectly pass); a `LeadCsvImport`-caching-only reversion would land at ≈356. Threshold tightened to **`< 310`** — strictly between the measured optimized result (299) and the smaller of the two independent regression totals (339) — so **either** half of the M6 optimization being reverted alone, not just a full reversion, now fails this test.

**Test results**:
- `LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php` + `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php` + `LeadImportUploadTest.php` + `LeadImportBroadcastTest.php`: **49/49 passed**, 148 assertions (includes the new multi-chunk-boundary test, the tightened query-budget test, and a new dedicated `LeadScoringField::Status`/`SourceType` regression test proving `setRelation()` provides correct data to the two scoring fields this change actually touches — a stronger guard than the pre-existing `HasEmail`-based scoring test, which never reads either relation).
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression, re-run three times across this closure's phases): **1029/1032 passed**, the 2 failures being the pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions (same baseline established throughout this session's prior closures), plus 1 pre-existing IMAP-extension skip — neither touched.
- Pint: clean, every run. PHPStan on the three touched production files: **0 errors before and 0 errors after** (re-measured via `git stash`, independently confirmed multiple times) — no new diagnostic.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Imports/LeadCsvImport.php`, `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php`, `app/Services/LeadScoringService.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/CreateLeadFromImportTest.php`. Five files total. **No migration, no config file, no queue/chunk architecture change, no new or modified lock primitive.**

**Explicitly confirmed unchanged at closure** (verified via direct `git diff` removed-lines inspection, not assumed): the tenant `sharedLock()` and its transaction boundary; the `leadStatuses` resolution under that lock; `Lead::nextIdentifier()`'s own locking (invoked internally by `Lead::create()`, file not touched); in-file and existing-Lead duplicate-detection queries and their ordering; `LeadImportRow`/`LeadImport` counter bookkeeping (`recordRow()`); the deliberate no-`LeadReceived`-per-row design; tag resolution; currency resolution; `LeadScoringRule` lookup/evaluation/logging; assignee resolution; `chunkSize()`/`registerEvents()`/`failed()` (queue/chunk configuration and retry semantics).

**Explicitly excluded from this closure's scope** (per the approved Architecture Brief, none implemented): tag catalog preloading, currency catalog preloading, `LeadScoringRule` preloading/caching, any change to duplicate-detection logic, any change to identifier-generation/locking, any queue architecture change (still plain `ShouldQueue` chaining, unchanged), M9, L3, L4. Raw HTTP request/database infrastructure concerns (server config, connection pooling, network latency) were never part of M6's scope and remain unaddressed by this closure, consistent with L2's own closure above drawing the same infrastructure/application boundary.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. M9, L3, and L4 remain open and unaddressed by this closure. The pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and IMAP-extension skip remain unrelated, pre-existing, and were not addressed here.

### L3 — CSV Export `assignedUser` Null-Safety — **FALSE POSITIVE / CLOSED**

Original forensic audit finding `L3` — one of the four items (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) carried forward as not-yet-approved-for-implementation as of the audit-status reconciliation performed earlier this session (L2 and M6 have since been closed above; this closes L3). Original wording flagged the expression `$lead->assignedUser->name ?? ...` in the CSV export path as a possible null-safety/runtime-warning risk. Evaluated via a dedicated Inspect → False-Positive Closure Verification sequence — does **not** proceed to Architecture Brief / Implementation / Testing / Final Verification, because Inspection itself disproved the premise.

**Exact suspected location**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index::export()`, `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php:1321`:
```php
$lead->assignedUser->name ?? 'Unassigned',
```
inside the `chunkById(200, ...)` streaming-CSV row callback.

**Why `assignedUser` can legitimately be null**: `Lead::assignedUser()` is a plain `belongsTo(TenantUser::class, 'assigned_to')` with no `withDefault()`. `leads.assigned_to` is `nullable()` and `nullOnDelete()` against `tenant_users` (`database/migrations/2026_08_26_130000_create_lead_management_tables.php:44`). Null is a real, reachable business state, not an edge case: a newly received lead is left unassigned whenever a tenant's distribution mode is manual or no eligible assignee exists; `LeadAssignmentService::unassign()` explicitly writes `'assigned_to' => null`; and `App\Services\LeadReportingService` treats `whereNull('assigned_to')` as its own first-class "unassigned leads" reporting bucket.

**Disproven — PHP 8.3.31 null-coalescing semantics, already-eager-loaded relation, no N+1**: the `??` operator uses `isset()`-style traversal, not a normal property read, so `$lead->assignedUser->name ?? 'Unassigned'` can never raise the "attempt to read property on null" warning even when `assignedUser` is null — confirmed by a direct language-semantics reproduction on the project's exact PHP 8.3.31 runtime. `assignedUser` is already present in `export()`'s own `->with([...])` eager-load list (`Index.php:1294`), so `relationLoaded('assignedUser')` is `true` for every row before the chunk callback runs regardless of whether the loaded value is a model or null — no lazy-load trigger exists. Independently, direct inspection of the installed Laravel 13.x source (`vendor/laravel/framework/.../Relations/BelongsTo.php:78-84`) confirmed `BelongsTo::getResults()` short-circuits to a default (null) with **zero query** whenever the foreign key itself is null, so even an un-eager-loaded access would not amplify query count. Null-safety and N+1/performance were evaluated as two genuinely distinct concerns per this session's instructions, and neither exists here — they were never conflated by assuming one implies the other.

**Existing test coverage, re-run and confirmed passing (16/16, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadsExportTest.php`)**: `the export writes each lead's exact csv row, including relationship-derived columns, without lazy-loading them` (assigned-lead case under `Model::preventLazyLoading()`) and, directly on point, `the export writes "Unassigned" for a lead with no assigned user` — an exact, pre-existing, already-passing test of this finding's null case. Chunk-boundary correctness (250 leads, no dupes/gaps), the bounded `chunkById(200, ...)` query-paging test, and both tenant-isolation tests (cross-tenant leak, non-admin own-leads-only export) were also confirmed unaffected.

**Live Postgres reproduction (no repository files modified)**: a full-app-boot script against the real dev Postgres connection, wrapped in an explicit `DB::beginTransaction()`/`DB::rollBack()` that was never committed (confirmed zero residual rows afterward), created an assigned lead, a never-assigned lead, and a lead whose `TenantUser` assignee was deleted *after* assignment — the real `nullOnDelete()` constraint was confirmed to fire on live Postgres, flipping `assigned_to` from the deleted user's ULID to `NULL` via `refresh()`. Eager-loading exactly as `export()` does and evaluating the exact `Index.php:1321` expression under `Model::preventLazyLoading()` for all three rows produced: `'Unassigned'` for both null cases, **0** additional queries, and no exception or warning of any kind.

**No additional test was added**: current coverage (the assigned case under `preventLazyLoading()`, the exact-output unassigned case, chunk-boundary correctness, and tenant isolation) already exercises every state `assigned_to` can legitimately hold and the exact expression at line 1321. The deleted-user path is state-equivalent to the already-tested null case (`assigned_to IS NULL` either way), and no code is changing that a new regression test would protect — adding one here would be documentation-only, which this closure explicitly does not do.

**No separate N+1/performance finding was opened.** The Impact Analysis explicitly distinguished the null-safety question from a possible query-amplification question and confirmed, with direct evidence (eager loading present, `BelongsTo` short-circuit on null FK, live 0-additional-query measurement), that no N+1 exists for `assignedUser` in this path — this is not a deferred or downgraded finding, it is a fully closed non-issue.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: no production code was changed — `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` and `app/Models/Lead.php` are byte-for-byte unchanged from before this finding was opened (confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` showing no drift throughout Inspection and Closure Verification); no test file was changed — `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadsExportTest.php` is unmodified and its full 16-test suite re-run and passing; no migration, config, or other finding in this section was touched.

**This finding is CLOSED as a FALSE POSITIVE.** Do not reopen without new evidence that the installed PHP/Laravel version's null-coalescing or `BelongsTo` short-circuit behavior has changed (e.g., a framework/language upgrade), or that a different, not-yet-identified code path accesses `assignedUser` without eager loading and without the `??` guard. M9 and L4 remain open and unaddressed by this closure.

### M9 — Manual Lead Auto-Assignment — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Original forensic audit finding `M9` — one of the four items (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) carried forward as not-yet-approved-for-implementation as of the audit-status reconciliation performed earlier this session (L2, M6, and L3 have since been closed above; this closes M9). Original wording identified an open behavior where manually created and/or CSV-imported leads do not follow the same auto-assignment + notification flow used by inbound lead creation. Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Product/Architecture Decision → User Approval → Final Architecture Plan → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Inspection findings, confirmed against current code and currently-passing tests**: manual and CSV lead creation both never fire `LeadReceived` (deliberate for CSV, per the M6-adjacent per-row-fan-out reasoning already documented in `CreateLeadFromImport::processRow()`; equally deliberate for manual, per the matching comment in `CreateManualLead::handle()`), so both were structurally unable to ever reach `LeadAssignmentService::autoAssign()` — its only caller in the entire codebase was the `AutoAssignLead` listener on `LeadReceived`. Both paths already had a working, direct-call scoring workaround (bypassing the event for `computeFor()`); assignment had no equivalent workaround. A related discovery, documented but explicitly out of this closure's scope: `Tenant.lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` have no admin-facing UI anywhere in the repository, so `Automatic` mode is currently reachable only via direct database/tinker intervention, not through the application itself.

**Product decision — narrowed scope, approved by the user**: rather than converging all of manual, CSV, notification, and board-refresh behavior at once, the approved scope is deliberately the smallest coherent slice — **manual-lead auto-assignment only**. CSV auto-assignment, any new notification mechanism (general or summary), real-time board-refresh broadcasting, and the `lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` admin UI were all explicitly evaluated in the Product/Architecture Decision Brief and explicitly declined for this closure, not silently deferred without consideration.

**Fix — one `if/else` branch, reusing the existing, unmodified `autoAssign()`**: in `CreateManualLead::handle()`, the previously unconditional `if ($assignee) { $this->assignmentService->assign($lead, $assignee, $createdBy); }` gained an `else` branch calling `$this->assignmentService->autoAssign($lead);` — positioned exactly where the explicit-assignment call already sat, inside the same pre-existing outer `DB::transaction()`, immediately before the existing (unmodified) `computeFor()` call. Approved exact behavior, all verified:
- **Explicit assignee always wins**: the `if`/`else` is mutually exclusive by construction — `assign()` and `autoAssign()` can never both run for the same lead. Verified deterministically (not by chance): the explicitly-selected assignee in the precedence test is a Client Admin, structurally outside `LeadAssignmentService::eligibleUsers()`'s `user_type = ClientUser` filter, so `autoAssign()` could not have produced that outcome even accidentally; a separate sole-eligible round-robin candidate was asserted as explicitly *not* the final assignee.
- **Blank assignee + `Automatic` mode → `autoAssign()`**: verified end-to-end via the real Livewire `saveLead()` path.
- **Blank assignee + `Manual` mode (today's default for every real tenant) → remains unassigned**: produced entirely by `autoAssign()`'s own pre-existing, unmodified guard clause (`lead_distribution_mode !== Automatic` → immediate return, zero writes) — not new logic.
- **Client User self-assignment unchanged**: `Index::saveLead()`'s existing `$assignee = $user` line for non-admins means `$assignee` is never null for a Client User, so the new `else` branch is structurally unreachable on that path — confirmed by the pre-existing, unmodified `'a non-admin with create permission is auto-assigned the manual lead they create'` test re-passing.

**No new notification mechanism — the existing `LeadAssignedNotification` is simply reachable a second way**: `autoAssign()` → `assign()` → the same pre-existing `DB::afterCommit(fn () => event(new LeadAssigned(...)))` → `SendLeadAssignedNotification`, identical to what explicit assignment already produces. This is not a new notification class or a new dispatch site; it is the same mechanism, now reachable via an automatic pick in addition to a human pick. **`LeadReceived` remains intentionally undispatched** for manual leads (directly proven by a new test under `Automatic` mode, the scenario most likely to regress this), so `SendNewLeadNotification` (the general "new lead" bell notification) and the `lead.received` real-time broadcast both remain unreachable, exactly as before this closure — **no general new-lead notification and no board-refresh behavior were added**, per the approved decision.

**CSV explicitly unchanged and excluded**: `app/Imports/LeadCsvImport.php` and `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php` were not touched — confirmed absent from the diff; CSV rows with a blank `assignee_email` continue to remain unassigned even under `Automatic` mode, exactly as the pre-existing, unmodified test `'a blank assignee_email leaves the lead unassigned, never auto-distributed'` continues to assert. No CSV auto-assignment and no CSV notification (per-row or summary) were implemented.

**Webhook and email paths unchanged**: `LeadWebhookController.php` and `ProcessEmailLeadSource.php` were not touched — both continue to fire `LeadReceived` exactly as before, unaffected by this closure.

**`LeadAssignmentService.php` unchanged**: confirmed zero diff throughout Implementation and Final Verification — `autoAssign()`'s existing mode-gating, round-robin/least-loaded/random picking, lock-and-reselect fairness protocol, and `eligibleUsers()` tenant/permission scoping are all reused exactly as-is, with no redesign.

**Transaction and failure semantics preserved, not redesigned**: the new branch sits inside the exact same, unmodified outer `DB::transaction()` `CreateManualLead::handle()` already used; `autoAssign()`'s own nested `DB::transaction()` (and `assign()`'s further-nested one inside that) become savepoints of that outer transaction, the same nesting shape already proven safe in production by `Index::applyBulkAssign()`'s existing, unmodified call to `assign()` from inside its own open transaction. No try/catch was introduced around `autoAssign()` — a failure inside it propagates and rolls back the entire manual lead creation, symmetric with how an explicit-assignment failure already behaves today (proven by the pre-existing, unmodified, re-passing test `'a failure partway through assign() rolls back every write already made in that transaction'`); no new failure policy was invented.

**Tenant isolation preserved**: `autoAssign()`'s `eligibleUsers()` tenant/module/permission scoping is entirely pre-existing and unmodified; no new `TenantUser` lookup path was introduced by this closure; no cross-tenant assignment possibility exists.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateManualLead.php` (one `if`/`else` branch, 2 lines added, nothing else touched), `tests/Feature/Leads/ManualLeadCreationTest.php` (4 new tests plus 4 new `use` imports, all 22 pre-existing tests in the file left untouched). Two files total. **No migration, no config file, no `LeadAssignmentService.php` change, no CSV file change, no webhook/email file change.**

**Test results**:
- `ManualLeadCreationTest.php` + `LeadAssignmentTest.php` + `LeadScoringTriggersTest.php` + `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php`, run together: **82/82 passed**, 238 assertions, 0 failures, 0 skips — re-confirmed independently at both Implementation and Final Verification.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression, re-run at both Implementation and Final Verification): **1033/1036 passed**, 2830 assertions, 1 skipped — the 2 failures being the same pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions established throughout this session's prior closures (independently reconfirmed via `git stash` to fail identically against unmodified `main`), the 1 skip being the known pre-existing IMAP-extension skip (`FetchEmailLeadsTest.php`) — neither touched, neither caused by this closure.
- Pint: clean, every run.
- PHPStan on `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateManualLead.php`: **0 errors before and 0 errors after** (re-measured via `git stash`, independently confirmed at both Implementation and Final Verification) — no new diagnostic.
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed unchanged at closure**: `LeadAssignmentService.php` (zero diff); `app/Imports/LeadCsvImport.php` and `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php` (CSV, zero diff); `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` and `app/Jobs/ProcessEmailLeadSource.php` (webhook/email, zero diff); the existing explicit-assignment branch, the existing `computeFor()` call and its position, the existing outer `DB::transaction()` boundary, and every pre-existing test in `ManualLeadCreationTest.php`, `LeadAssignmentTest.php`, `LeadScoringTriggersTest.php`, and `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php`.

**Explicitly excluded from this closure's scope** (per the approved Product/Architecture Decision Brief, none implemented): CSV auto-assignment (would require new real per-row lock-contention measurement at up-to-10,000-row scale, not performed); CSV notification, per-row or post-import-summary; a general new-lead notification for manually created leads; real-time board-refresh broadcasting for manual or CSV leads; the `lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` admin settings UI — explicitly identified as a separate, future, independently-scheduled feature, not a prerequisite this closure silently assumed; any redesign of `LeadAssignmentService`; L4.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. L4 remains open and unaddressed by this closure.

### L4 — Enum-at-Migration-Time CHECK Fragility — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Original forensic audit finding `L4` — the last of the four items (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) carried forward as not-yet-approved-for-implementation as of the audit-status reconciliation performed earlier this session (L2, M6, L3, and M9 have since been closed above; this closes L4, the final remaining open item). Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence. **Finding classification: CONFIRMED ARCHITECTURAL RISK. Priority: LOW.**

**Exact defect class**: an already-provisioned PostgreSQL database's CHECK constraint (Laravel's `enum()` migration helper emulates a native enum as `varchar check (col in (...))` on both Postgres and SQLite — confirmed identical in shape via direct grammar source inspection) can drift behind its corresponding PHP enum class when a new/changed enum case is deployed without a companion migration updating that constraint on every already-existing database. This is structurally distinct from a bug in any single migration file: the migration that originally created the column re-derives its CHECK list from the *live* enum every time it runs, so a freshly-migrated database (every automated test, via `RefreshDatabase`) can never reproduce "an already-provisioned database whose constraint predates a later enum change" — only a real, previously-migrated environment can.

**Historical incident, not theoretical**: `App\Enums\LeadSourceType::CsvImport` was added without a companion constraint migration; the full SQLite-based test suite passed regardless (per the mechanism above), and the drift was caught only by a manual smoke test against the real dev Postgres database — documented directly in `database/migrations/2026_09_12_080002_add_csv_import_to_lead_sources_type_check.php`'s own docblock. This confirmed the failure mode is real, not speculative, and that the current SQLite-only automated test suite (and current CI, which has no PostgreSQL service container — confirmed by reading `.github/workflows/tests.yml`) is structurally unable to detect a recurrence, by construction, not by an accidental gap.

**Current verified state, re-confirmed multiple times through Implementation and Final Verification**: **20 live enum-backed CHECK constraints, across 10 Lead-Management-area models/tables, backed by 15 PHP enum classes — zero drift, all 20 matching.**

**Approved mitigation — read-only, additive guardrail (Option C from the Architecture Brief)**:
- `App\Services\EnumConstraintVerifier` — discovers enum-backed columns from a fixed, explicit, 10-model Lead-Management set (`Tenant`, `LeadSource`, `Lead`, `Conversation`, `Message`, `LeadDuplicateLink`, `LeadAttachment`, `LeadConsent`, `LeadConsentEvent`, `WebsiteFormField`) via each model's own `getCasts()` — reusing the exact resolved cast list Eloquent itself already uses to hydrate attributes, not a hand-maintained mirror of it, and deliberately not a reflection scan of every class under `app/Models`. For each discovered `(table, column, enum)` triple, inspects the real PostgreSQL catalog directly (`pg_constraint`/`pg_get_constraintdef()`, scoped by `conrelid = ?::regclass`), matching the governing CHECK constraint by a **word-boundary regex on the column name** — deliberately not Laravel's default `{table}_{column}_check` naming convention, so a differently-named constraint is still found correctly and a genuinely ambiguous schema (two CHECK constraints touching the same column) is reported, never guessed past. Parses PostgreSQL's own canonical `col = ANY (ARRAY[...])` rendering of the constraint (confirmed, against the real dev database, to be identical in shape regardless of whether the originating migration used Laravel's `enum()` blueprint or a raw `DB::statement()` `CHECK (col IN (...))`), and compares the extracted values against the enum's current `cases()` as **unordered sets**, reporting values missing from the DB constraint and values present in the DB but absent from the enum as two separate, non-conflated outcomes. Two columns sharing one enum class (e.g. `MessageChannel` on both `conversations.channel` and `messages.channel`) are checked fully independently.
- `php artisan schema:verify-enum-constraints` (`App\Console\Commands\VerifyEnumConstraints`) — a thin, read-only CLI wrapper with a deliberate three-state exit-code contract, chosen specifically because a plain 0/1 pair cannot distinguish "verified and clean" from "not verified at all": **0** = PostgreSQL reached, every mapping matched; **1** = drift, a missing/ambiguous/malformed constraint, or a connection failure — something is genuinely wrong or could not be checked; **2** = not applicable (the active connection isn't PostgreSQL) — never reported as exit 0, so a caller can never mistake "skipped" for "passed".
- `tests/Feature/Schema/EnumConstraintVerifierTest.php` — pure, DB-free unit tests of the parsing/comparison logic (both made `public static`, mirroring `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()`'s existing "public static for direct unit testing" convention) plus real-connection tests (including `ReflectionMethod`-based tests of the private catalog-lookup helpers, matching the existing precedent in `tests/Unit/ProcessEmailLeadSourceBodyExtractionTest.php`) that recover real PostgreSQL credentials directly from `.env` via `Dotenv::parse()` (never from `env()`, which `phpunit.xml`'s `DB_DATABASE=":memory:"` override was confirmed to clobber process-wide for every registered connection, not just SQLite's) and skip cleanly wherever a real Postgres connection genuinely isn't reachable — never a false pass.

**Verification**:
- Real PostgreSQL, run directly against the actual dev database, re-confirmed twice during Final Verification: **20/20 mappings matched, 0 missing constraints, 0 ambiguous, 0 malformed, 0 missing enum values, 0 extra DB values, exit code 0.**
- Forced non-Postgres connection: explicit `SKIPPED / NOT APPLICABLE`, exit code **2** — never a false pass. Forced unreachable-connection attempt: distinct `connection_error`, exit code **1**.
- `tests/Feature/Schema/EnumConstraintVerifierTest.php`: **17/17 passed**, 34 assertions, 0 skipped — re-run fresh, twice, at Final Verification.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression, re-run twice fresh at Final Verification): **1033/1036 passed**, 2830 assertions, 1 skipped — the 2 failures being the same pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions established throughout this session's prior closures, the 1 skip being the known pre-existing IMAP-extension skip; a one-off `KanbanBoardTest` failure observed once during Implementation was independently reconfirmed, by isolated re-run (3/3 passing) and by direct code-path inspection, to be the same pre-existing factory-randomness flake already documented in this section's `resendMessage()` closure, with zero connection to any L4 file.
- Pint: clean, every run. PHPStan on the two new production files (`EnumConstraintVerifier.php`, `VerifyEnumConstraints.php`): **0 errors** — no pre-existing baseline exists for new files, so this is reported as the exact current result, not a fabricated before/after comparison.
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Read-only, by construction and by direct verification**: a literal search of both new production files for every write/DDL SQL verb (`insert`, `update`, `delete`, `alter table`, `create table`, `drop table`, `create type`, `drop type`) returned zero matches. The one schema-mutating statement anywhere in this closure's changeset lives inside the test file's ambiguous-constraint test, wrapped in an explicit, uncommitted `beginTransaction()`/`rollBack()` — confirmed directly against the real database, after the test run, that `leads` still carries exactly its one original `leads_priority_check` constraint with no leftover artifact.

**No migration, schema, config, or CI change of any kind**: no migration file was added or modified; no PHP enum, model, or config file was touched; `.github/workflows/tests.yml` is unmodified — current CI remains SQLite-only, exactly as before this closure. The existing companion-migration convention (write a dedicated `ALTER TABLE ... DROP/ADD CONSTRAINT` migration whenever an enum case changes) remains fully in place and is not superseded by this guardrail — the guardrail *verifies* that convention was followed, it does not replace it. The command is designed to be run manually today, or wired into a future deployment pipeline later — neither is required for this closure.

**Explicitly scoped — what this guardrail does and does not cover**: it detects exactly one defect class — a PHP enum's current case set diverging from an already-provisioned PostgreSQL CHECK constraint governing the corresponding column — the `LeadSourceType::CsvImport` incident's exact shape. It does **not** detect, and makes no claim to detect, unrelated schema/migration drift such as index drift, foreign-key drift, arbitrary column type changes, or data-level corruption. The current, healthy, fully-consistent database state confirmed by this closure is a snapshot at closure time, not a permanent guarantee — the value of this guardrail is that a future recurrence is now something a developer can check on demand, not something that can only be caught by memory.

**Exact files added**: `app/Services/EnumConstraintVerifier.php`, `app/Console/Commands/VerifyEnumConstraints.php`, `tests/Feature/Schema/EnumConstraintVerifierTest.php`. Three files total, all new — no existing file was modified.

**Explicitly excluded from this closure's scope** (per the approved Architecture Brief, evaluated and deliberately rejected as disproportionate to a Low-priority, single-historical-incident, currently-zero-drift finding): native PostgreSQL `ENUM` type migration (would also break this codebase's own documented SQLite-test-portability decision); catalog-table conversion of any of the 15 remaining enums (the one enum that genuinely warranted this, `LeadStatus`, was already converted before this session, independent of L4); any migration rewrite; any auto-fix/auto-remediation behavior; a PostgreSQL CI service container; any broader schema redesign.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression, or unless the guardrail itself is later found to miss a real drift case within its stated scope. No other audit finding remains open — L4 was the last of the four (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) carried forward from the original audit-status reconciliation, and all four are now closed.

### SEC-1 — Cross-tenant LeadSource / SourceLeads Data Exposure — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not from the original carried-forward audit list (`M6, M9, L2–L4`, all already closed above) — discovered during a separate, fresh, full forensic audit of the entire Lead Management module conducted after that list's closure. **Only this one finding (SEC-1) from that fresh audit is being closed here** — every other finding surfaced by that audit remains open and unaddressed by this closure; this section does not represent, and must not be read as, closure of the new audit as a whole. Closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval sequence. **Severity: CRITICAL. Confidence: HIGH.**

**Finding**: any authenticated `TenantUser` (Client Admin or Client User) holding only their own tenant's ordinary `lead-management`/`view` permission could view — and bulk-export via XLSX — another tenant's Facebook Lead Ads leads, including every dynamic Instant Form answer, simply by visiting `/leads/sources/{leadSource}` with another tenant's `LeadSource` ULID. Empirically reproduced end-to-end through the real Livewire/HTTP/routing pipeline before any fix, confirming real cross-tenant data (name, email, and full lead row) rendered to the requesting tenant.

**Root cause**:
- `SourceLeads::mount()` authorized only at the class level (`Gate::authorize('viewAny', Lead::class)`, which has no reference to the specific `$leadSource` at all) instead of the instance level — the sole outlier among the module's route-bound Livewire components; `Leads\Show::mount()` and `LeadImports\Show::mount()` both already used correct instance-level tenant checks, confirmed by direct comparison.
- `SourceLeads::baseQuery()` filtered only by `source_id`, with no `tenant_id` predicate anywhere in the query chain that `leads()`, `fieldColumns()`, and `downloadXlsx()` all derive from.
- An already-existing, already-correct policy method, `LeadSourcePolicy::view()` (`$user->tenant_id === $leadSource->tenant_id && $user->hasModulePermission('lead-management', 'view')`), sat completely unused anywhere in the codebase — the fix did not need to write new authorization logic, only wire up what already existed.

**Remediation**:
- `mount()`: `Gate::authorize('viewAny', Lead::class)` → `Gate::authorize('view', $leadSource)`, reusing `LeadSourcePolicy::view()` unmodified, mirroring `Leads\Show::mount()`'s own established `Gate::authorize('view', $lead)` pattern.
- `baseQuery()`: added an explicit `->where('tenant_id', Auth::user()->tenant_id)` predicate, matching the same discipline already used by `Index::filteredQuery()` — a defense-in-depth guarantee independent of `mount()`'s gate, since `downloadXlsx()` and `fieldColumns()` both derive from this same query.
- `fieldColumns()`: added `->orderBy('id')` — a narrow, separately-approved follow-up fix for a patch-caused side effect: adding the `tenant_id` predicate altered PostgreSQL's query-plan-dependent implicit row order for this one `orderBy()`-less query, intermittently breaking its documented "first-seen column order" guarantee. `Lead`'s ULID primary key is time-sortable, so this restores deterministic first-seen order with no behavior change beyond removing the flake.

**Verification**:
- Real cross-tenant HTTP reproduction (two genuine tenants, a real Facebook Lead Ads `LeadSource` + `Lead` belonging to Tenant B, a real Client Admin authenticated as Tenant A): pre-fix, mount succeeded and Tenant B's lead data was returned and rendered; post-fix, the identical request returns **HTTP 403** with Tenant B's data absent from the response body.
- `baseQuery()` invoked directly via reflection (bypassing `mount()`'s gate entirely, proving the defense-in-depth guarantee holds independent of it): **0** Tenant B rows returned.
- The XLSX export's underlying data source (the same `baseQuery()`): **0** Tenant B rows; the actual generated spreadsheet file confirmed to contain only its header row.
- Same-tenant Client Admin and Client User access confirmed unchanged and fully preserved (the pre-existing, unmodified tests covering both roles' spreadsheet access continue to pass).
- `tests/Feature/Leads/FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php`: **40/40 passed**, 102 assertions, stable across **5 consecutive runs** (the `fieldColumns()` ordering flake introduced by the `baseQuery()` change, and independently confirmed via `git stash` comparison to be absent on unmodified code, no longer reproduces after the `orderBy('id')` fix).
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` (broader regression, re-run twice): **1037/1040 passed**, 2836 assertions, 1 skipped — the 2 failures being the same pre-existing, unrelated `LeadsIndexListenerTest` `getListeners()` mapping assertions established throughout this module's closure history, the 1 skip being the known pre-existing IMAP-extension skip — neither touched, neither caused by this change.
- Pint: clean, every run. PHPStan on `SourceLeads.php`: 4 errors both before and after the `orderBy('id')` addition (unchanged by that specific line); the one net-new error versus the pre-SEC-1 baseline (3 errors) is the exact same pre-existing `Auth::user()->tenant_id`-on-a-union-type pattern already present twice elsewhere, unmodified, in this same file — not a new class of diagnostic.
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**No Platform Admin behavior is affected**: this route (`leads/sources/{leadSource}`) sits under the tenant `web` guard (`TenantUser`) exclusively; Platform Admins operate under a fully separate guard and route file (`routes/admin.php`) and cannot reach this URL as themselves — confirmed directly, not assumed.

**Related-pattern search, scoped narrowly to this one finding's structural shape** (route/model binding → authenticated user → class-level policy/permission → instance query, omitting tenant ownership), performed within the Lead Management module only: exhaustively checked every route-bound-model Livewire `mount()` and every route-bound-model controller action in the module (`Leads\Show::mount()`, `LeadImports\Show::mount()`, `LeadAttachmentController::download()`, `Index::viewLead()` and its sibling ID-taking actions). **No other candidate was found** — every one of them already uses a correct, tenant-aware instance check. `SourceLeads` was a genuinely isolated outlier, not evidence of a broader systemic gap.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/SourceLeads.php` (`mount()`, `baseQuery()`, `fieldColumns()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php` (4 new regression tests appended; no existing test modified). Two files total.

**Explicitly excluded from this closure's scope**: **Option D** — a deeper, framework-level fix ensuring tenant-aware route-model binding is correctly effective at bind time for every tenant-scoped model in the application (e.g. via middleware-priority changes or explicit `Route::bind()` closures), which would close this entire *class* of potential future bug in one place rather than this one component — was evaluated in the Architecture/Decision Brief and explicitly **not implemented**, given its materially higher regression risk (app-wide, not localized) and the genuine uncertainty this investigation surfaced about exact Laravel middleware-ordering internals. It remains a separate, future, independently-scheduled architecture consideration, not bundled into this critical fix. `LeadSourcePolicy`, `LeadPolicy`, `BelongsToTenant`, `FiltersLeadsQuery`, routes, middleware, migrations, and every other closed finding in this section were confirmed untouched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. Every other finding surfaced by the fresh forensic audit this was discovered in remains open and is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) scheduling gap (the observation first flagged during M4's own closure, not an independently-numbered finding) — CLOSED (see §23); `Schedule::command('leads:detect-duplicates')->daily()` added to `routes/console.php`, matching the `leads:recalculate-scores` convention exactly, command logic itself intentionally unchanged. Platform LeadManagement Settings deleteStatus()/deleteCurrency() TOCTOU race (a freshly-discovered observation, same bug class as C1/tenant deleteSource(), not an independently-numbered finding) — CLOSED (see §23); DB::transaction() + lockForUpdate() on the parent LeadStatus/Currency row in each method independently, mirroring C1, with FOR UPDATE independently confirmed against the real pgsql connection; the LeadStatusLog missing-check gap discovered alongside it was later closed as its own separate finding. Platform LeadManagement Settings deleteStatus() — missing LeadStatusLog dependent check (discovered during and deliberately deferred from the TOCTOU closure above, not an independently-numbered finding) — CLOSED (see §23); one additional exists() check against lead_status_logs.old_status_id/new_status_id, checked first, inside the same already-established transaction/lock boundary; genuine fail-then-pass proof via a real SQLite FK restrict-violation QueryException. Tenant LeadCustomFields\Index::delete() TOCTOU race (a freshly-discovered observation, same bug class as C1/tenant deleteSource()/Platform Settings, not an independently-numbered finding) — CLOSED (see §23); DB::transaction() + lockForUpdate() on the tenant-scoped LeadCustomFieldDefinition row, mirroring the three prior fixes exactly, with FOR UPDATE independently confirmed against the real pgsql connection; hasValues()/isReferencedByWebsiteForm() confirmed already complete and left untouched. LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown() — check-then-fetch race on a deleted LeadSource (M10) — CLOSED (see §23); LeadSource rows fetched before the aggregate count query rather than after, race eliminated by the confirmed cascadeOnDelete() invariant on leads.source_id, not by a transaction (PostgreSQL READ COMMITTED does not give multiple statements one shared snapshot); genuine fail-then-pass proof via a real DB::listen() interception reproducing an uncaught ErrorException against the original order. Leads\Index::export() — CSV/formula injection via unsanitized name/email (a freshly-discovered security finding, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — CLOSED (see §23); a private sanitizeCsvCell() helper prefixes a leading =/+/-/@/tab/CR with a single quote for name/email only, phone deliberately left unsanitized per approved architecture since its own validation regex makes genuine formula injection impossible there; genuine fail-then-pass proof verified twice, real streamed-CSV-byte verification performed. `Leads\Index::delete()`/`Leads\Show::delete()` — missing server-side enforcement of the deletable-source-type restriction (the previously-deferred "`Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap," not an independently-numbered audit ID) — CLOSED (see §23); a new canonical `LeadSourceType::isIndividuallyDeletable()` method is the single source of truth, enforced server-side in both delete() methods and adopted (behavior-preserving) by the already-correct performBulkDelete()/Index table UI, with matching UX-only gating added to Show's two Delete-button render sites; genuine fail-then-pass proof performed twice, independently, including neutralizing the canonical rule itself to prove every call site is genuinely wired through it. `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::toggleLeadStatus()` — TOCTOU race causing silent pipeline-stranded leads (a freshly-discovered observation, same bug class as C1/tenant deleteSource()/Platform Settings/LeadCustomFields but with no FK backstop at all, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — CLOSED (see §23); a Tenant-row shared/exclusive locking protocol (`FOR UPDATE` for the rare removal, `FOR SHARE` for all 7 status-assigning writers) coordinated via `DB::transaction()`, with `Index::applyBulkStatusChange()` additionally corrected mid-verification to use a nested `DB::transaction()`/`SAVEPOINT` per lead so the coordination lock could be held for the whole bounded batch without narrowing that method's own pre-existing per-lead failure-isolation guarantee; real PostgreSQL `FOR SHARE`/`FOR UPDATE` semantics and two real two-process races (including the batch/savepoint case) independently verified against the actual database connection; historical stranded-lead diagnostic found 0 affected rows. `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::toggleCurrency()` — TOCTOU race allowing a tenant to reach zero enabled currencies (the previously-deferred lower-severity sibling of the `toggleLeadStatus()` closure above, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — CLOSED (see §23); confirmed the sole application-level currency-removal writer (no multi-writer coordination needed, unlike `toggleLeadStatus()`), a single exclusive `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` around the removal branch only, addition branch left unlocked; genuine fail-then-pass proof performed twice independently, plus two real two-process Postgres races (both orderings) using the actual production method; historical zero-currency diagnostic found 0 affected tenants. `App\Actions\Platform\AssignModuleToTenant::handle()` — concurrent duplicate module assignment (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — evaluated via Inspect → Impact Analysis → User Decision and explicitly **DEFERRED / ACCEPTABLE CONCURRENT CONFLICT — NO IMPLEMENTATION REQUIRED** (see §23); genuinely reproduced via a real two-process PostgreSQL race, the losing transaction rolls back completely with zero partial/orphaned state, the winning transaction commits correctly, and the sole caller already catches the resulting `QueryException` with a controlled toast — accepted as consistent with this codebase's own unlocked `firstOrCreate()`/`updateOrCreate()` convention for equivalent low-contention provisioning races, and explicitly distinguished from `toggleLeadStatus()`/`toggleCurrency()` above (both of which involved a silent invariant violation, not a caught/rolled-back conflict); this disposition also supersedes and fully accounts for `AssignModuleToTenant::seedDefaultCurrency()`'s duplicate-attach race, previously named as a separate deferred observation in the `toggleCurrency()` closure. `App\Actions\Tenant\RecordLeadConsent::handle()` — hypothesized concurrent consent race (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — evaluated via Inspect → Impact Analysis and classified **FALSE POSITIVE — NO IMPLEMENTATION REQUIRED** (see §23); the installed Laravel version's `updateOrCreate()`/`createOrFirst()` was confirmed, via vendor-source inspection and a genuine two-process PostgreSQL reproduction, to already catch the concurrent unique-constraint collision internally (protected by a real savepoint around the surrounding transaction), so neither transaction throws; final state verified correct (exactly one `lead_consents` row, both `lead_consent_events` rows preserved); no production code, test, or documentation-of-a-fix was needed — explicitly distinguished from the genuine races fixed above (`toggleLeadStatus()`, `toggleCurrency()`) and from the genuinely-real, separately-deferred `AssignModuleToTenant::handle()` race, since those involve pivot-table writes with no equivalent native protection. DE1 (permanently undeletable LeadSource with conversation history), the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, and `LeadSources::save()`'s narrower pre-existing check-then-persist gap remain explicitly deferred pending separate decisions — not implemented, not scheduled. No other audit finding (M6, M9, L2–L4) has been approved for implementation. `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::resendMessage()` — duplicate resend / duplicate outbound delivery (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; persisted `messages.resend_message_id` (nullable, self-referencing, `nullOnDelete()`, same shape as `merged_into_lead_id`) + `DB::transaction()` + `Message::lockForUpdate()` enforce exactly one resend per Failed row, checked/set inside the same lock that creates the resend row, mirroring the `LeadCustomFieldDefinition::delete()`/Platform Settings/`toggleLeadStatus()`/`toggleCurrency()` "lock the row before a may-I-do-this check" idiom; genuine two-process PostgreSQL race independently re-verified twice (Implementation and Final Verification, fresh fixture each time) confirms exactly one resend row and zero duplicates; sequential (non-concurrent) duplicate resend also confirmed rejected; `sendMessage()` and `SendOutboundMessage` confirmed unchanged; PHPStan diagnostics on the two changed files unchanged (60/60) versus the pre-existing baseline. `App\Http\Controllers\Api\LeadWebhookController` — inbound webhook message redelivery / duplicate append (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; a new `alreadyRecordedMessage()` gate, keyed on `tenant_id` + `channel` + `external_message_id` (reusing the existing `messages_external_id_unique` index, no new table/column), checked as the first statement inside each channel's existing per-sender `withDedupeLock()` closure — before `existingLeadForConversation()`, the legacy fallback lookup, `append*Message()`, and `createLead()` — mirroring `processFacebookLeadAd()`'s own `alreadyProcessed` check exactly; null-external-id payloads bypass the gate unchanged, no identifier synthesized; genuine two-process PostgreSQL race independently re-verified twice (Implementation and Final Verification, fresh fixture each time) confirms exactly one Lead/one normalized `Message`/one legacy append with zero duplication even though the pre-existing sender lock alone (confirmed via `Illuminate\Cache\Lock::block()`'s own source) never durably prevented this; duplicate-attachment redelivery confirmed to create no second `LeadAttachment` and dispatch no second `DownloadLeadAttachment` job; `RecordInboundMessage`, `existingLeadForConversation()`, and `VerifyMetaWebhookSignature` confirmed unchanged; PHPStan diagnostics on the touched controller unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L2 (unbounded logical webhook message/entry processing per request, from the original forensic audit, previously carried forward as not-yet-approved alongside M6/M9/L3/L4) — **CLOSED / FIXED at the application level only**; the original finding's raw HTTP-body-size question is explicitly NOT resolved by this closure and remains a separate infrastructure/operational item outside repository scope; the application-level defect actually closed — unbounded logical message count per request, dynamically measured at 36KB/200 messages producing ~8s processing and 200 real Lead+Message rows — is fixed via a configurable total-per-request logical-message cap (`config/webhooks.php`, default 100, mirroring `Index::MAX_BULK_SELECTION`), checked before any per-message work in `facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()`, counting `messaging[]`/`value.messages[]` across all entries while excluding delivery/read receipts and WhatsApp `statuses[]`; over-limit requests are rejected in full with the existing HTTP 200 acknowledgement preserved, no partial processing; genuine real-Postgres boundary verification independently re-run twice confirms 100 accepted and 101 rejected with no off-by-one; the recently-closed inbound redelivery idempotency gate is unmodified and confirmed still correct in combination with the new cap; M9, L3, and L4 remain open, unaddressed by this closure. M6 (CSV import per-row N+1 / redundant database reads, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; confirmed a genuine performance/scalability and queue-worker resource-contention issue, not a correctness or security one, via real measurement (10 rows = 195 queries, 200 rows = 3,805 queries, linear scaling); fixed by lazily caching `LeadImport`/`Tenant`/`LeadSource` once per `ReadChunk` job (chunk-scoped, not a whole-import shared cache — verified against the installed `maatwebsite/excel` vendor source that each chunk is a separately serialized job) plus reusing already-resolved `source`/`status` relations for scoring via a new opt-in, default-`false` `computeFor($trustLoadedRelations)` parameter used only by CSV import; `TenantUser` deliberately left uncached (no delete guard exists for it, unlike the other three, which are provably undeletable for an import's lifetime) and confirmed still queried once per row at every measured scale including across a chunk boundary; measured reduction 24.1% (N=10) and 26.2% (N=200), independently re-confirmed three times; a real query-count regression test (threshold tightened from `<340` to `<310` after Final Verification identified and closed a blind spot that would have let a `computeFor()`-only rollback at ≈339 queries silently pass) now catches both halves of the optimization being independently reverted; tenant `sharedLock()`, `leadStatuses` resolution, `Lead::nextIdentifier()`, duplicate detection, `LeadImportRow`/`LeadImport` bookkeeping, and the no-`LeadReceived`-per-row design all confirmed unchanged via direct diff inspection; tag/currency/scoring-rule caching, duplicate-detection optimization, identifier-generation optimization, and queue architecture changes were explicitly excluded from scope and not implemented; PHPStan diagnostics on the three touched production files unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L3 (CSV export `assignedUser` null-safety, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FALSE POSITIVE**; `$lead->assignedUser->name ?? 'Unassigned'` (`Index.php:1321`) is unconditionally safe under PHP 8.3.31's isset-style `??` semantics, `assignedUser` is already eager-loaded by `export()`'s own `with()` so no lazy load or N+1 can occur (independently confirmed by `BelongsTo::getResults()`'s null-foreign-key short-circuit and a live Postgres reproduction showing 0 additional queries), and a pre-existing test already asserted the exact "Unassigned" output for a null-assignee lead; a live Postgres reproduction additionally confirmed the `nullOnDelete()`-after-user-deletion path resolves identically; no production code, test, or migration was changed. M9 and L4 remain open, unaddressed by this closure. M9 (manual/CSV leads skipping auto-assignment and notification, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED, narrowed to manual-lead auto-assignment only per approved product decision**; `CreateManualLead::handle()` gained one `if`/`else` branch calling the existing, unmodified `LeadAssignmentService::autoAssign()` when no explicit assignee was supplied, reusing the exact direct-service-call pattern already established for scoring; explicit assignment always wins (verified deterministically, not by chance), `Manual` mode (today's default for every real tenant) continues to no-op via `autoAssign()`'s own pre-existing guard, and Client User self-assignment is unaffected since the new branch is structurally unreachable on that path; no new notification mechanism was added — `LeadAssignedNotification` is simply reachable a second way through the same pre-existing `assign()` → `DB::afterCommit()` chain — and `LeadReceived` remains intentionally undispatched, so the general new-lead notification and real-time board-refresh broadcast remain unreachable exactly as before; CSV auto-assignment/notification, the `lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` admin UI, and any `LeadAssignmentService` redesign were explicitly evaluated and declined for this closure, not silently deferred; `LeadAssignmentService.php`, CSV (`LeadCsvImport.php`/`CreateLeadFromImport.php`), and webhook/email (`LeadWebhookController.php`/`ProcessEmailLeadSource.php`) all confirmed zero diff; focused tests 82/82 passed, broader Leads regression 1033/1036 passed with the same 2 pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and 1 known IMAP skip re-confirmed via `git stash`; PHPStan on the one touched production file unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L4 remains open, unaddressed by this closure. L4 (enum-at-migration-time CHECK fragility, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / CONFIRMED ARCHITECTURAL RISK, LOW PRIORITY, MITIGATED**; an already-provisioned PostgreSQL CHECK constraint can drift behind its PHP enum when a case changes without a companion migration — proven real once (`LeadSourceType::CsvImport`, caught only by a manual smoke test, per that migration's own docblock) and structurally invisible to the SQLite-only automated suite by construction, since a freshly-migrated test database always re-derives its constraint from the live enum; current state directly verified as 20/20 enum-backed constraints matching with zero drift; mitigated with a read-only, additive guardrail — `App\Services\EnumConstraintVerifier` (discovers mappings from a fixed 10-model Lead-Management set via `getCasts()`, inspects `pg_constraint`/`pg_get_constraintdef()` directly, matches by word-boundary column search rather than Laravel's default naming convention, compares values as unordered sets) plus `php artisan schema:verify-enum-constraints` (deliberate three-state exit code: 0 clean, 1 drift/error, 2 not-applicable — never a false pass) plus a focused Pest suite (17/17 passed); confirmed read-only by direct grep for every write/DDL verb (zero matches) and by re-confirming the one test-side schema mutation was inside an uncommitted, rolled-back transaction; no migration, enum, model, config, or CI file touched — current CI remains SQLite-only and the existing companion-migration convention is unchanged; native PostgreSQL ENUM and catalog-table conversion were evaluated and explicitly rejected as disproportionate; the guardrail is explicitly scoped to the one defect class (enum-vs-CHECK-constraint drift), not a general schema-drift detector. This was the last of the four (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) originally carried-forward findings — none remain open. SEC-1 (cross-tenant `LeadSource`/`SourceLeads` data exposure, discovered during a separate, fresh, full forensic audit of the module conducted after the above four were closed) — **CLOSED / FIXED, CRITICAL**; `SourceLeads::mount()`'s class-level `viewAny` check replaced with instance-level `Gate::authorize('view', $leadSource)` reusing the already-existing, previously-unused `LeadSourcePolicy::view()`, plus an explicit `tenant_id` predicate added to `baseQuery()` as an independent defense-in-depth guarantee covering `leads()`/`fieldColumns()`/`downloadXlsx()` alike, plus a narrow `orderBy('id')` follow-up in `fieldColumns()` to resolve a patch-caused, `git stash`-confirmed intermittent ordering flake; real end-to-end cross-tenant HTTP reproduction confirmed the exploit before the fix and confirmed HTTP 403 with zero Tenant B data after it; a narrowly-scoped related-pattern search within the Lead Management module found no other candidate; Option D (a deeper, framework-level tenant-aware route-binding fix) was evaluated and explicitly deferred as a separate future consideration, not implemented; `LeadSourcePolicy`, `LeadPolicy`, `BelongsToTenant`, routes, and middleware all confirmed untouched. **This closes only SEC-1 — every other finding from that same fresh forensic audit remains open and unaddressed.****
**Next required action: None active. Wait for explicit instruction before starting the next audit finding, or any further work.**
